| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| works/[id]/mockups: flag-gated, artist-only (must own both the work and the render), idempotent by render id; buyer-irrelevant except that promoted mockups appear as extra images on the artwork page | Yes | BLOCKED. Artist-only, checked in area D. |
| walls/render-quick: flag-gated, authenticated, quota-consumed with refund on failure; powers the polished render inside the customer wall sheet, which itself is open to guests for drag/preview and only asks for an account at Render | Yes, matches the "free to play, sign in to render" product intent | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| analytics/track: allow-listed event types only (venue_viewed_artist, artwork_view, profile_view), rate-limited, 60s visitor dedupe | Yes | WORKS. POST /api/analytics/track {"event_type":"artwork_view",…} returned 200 {"ok":true}, and profile and artwork view counters incremented across visits. |
| qr/[slug]: logs qr_scan with venue resolution (fresh display name from venue_profiles so renames do not require reprints), work id or legacy title, then 302s to /browse/[slug] with ref=qr, venue slug, signed attribution token (best-effort), venueName, work and size params | Yes; note a label carrying only `w=` (id) and no title lands on the profile without auto-opening that work, since the redirect only sets `work=` from the title | WORKS. Both QR scans landed in analytics_events as qr_scan; a scan with vs=testing-venue resolves the venue (earlier real rows carry venue_user_id and the display name "Testing Venue"), while the seed-only slug the-copper-kettle resolves to nothing because no venue_profiles row exists for it. |
| Buyer-visible effect of QR venue attribution: the "Seen in <venue>" banner and unchanged prices; the venue's cut comes out of the artist's share server-side and is never itemised to the buyer | Yes, correct presentation | DIFFERS. The attribution half works (wallplace:qr-context is written with the venue slug, name and signed token, and the checkout price was unchanged) but the buyer-visible "Seen in <venue>" banner does not render anywhere on the landing page. |

---

# C. Customer portal and account (User)

Scope: the registered customer portal (dashboard, saved, addresses, settings, messages entry), the shared account pages (/account/appeal, /account/email, /account/email/unsubscribe, /account/export, /account/security), CustomerPortalLayout, PortalGuard as it affects customers, AccountDangerZone, the Header notification bell and role switcher, and the backing APIs: /api/account (DELETE), /api/account/delete, /api/account/email-preferences, /api/account/export, /api/account/preferences, /api/account/roles, /api/customer-addresses (+/[id]), /api/notifications, /api/me/subscription, /api/terms/accept, /api/auth/resend-verification, /api/account/email/unsubscribe. All judgements are grounded in the source read on branch claude/wallplace-remediation-finish-42e4c1.

## Portal access and layout (/customer-portal/*, CustomerPortalLayout + PortalGuard) [registered customer]

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Every customer-portal page is wrapped in PortalGuard with allowedType "customer"; an unauthenticated visitor is redirected to /login via router.replace. | Yes | WORKS. Signing out and reloading /customer-portal redirects to /login; every portal page was reachable only while signed in. |
| A signed-in user of a different role (artist, venue, admin) gets an info toast ("This is the customer portal. Redirecting to your X portal.") and is redirected to their own portal via portalPathForRole. | Yes | BLOCKED. Not driven; the artist and venue accounts were tested in their own portals rather than pointed at this one. |
| A signed-in user whose email is unconfirmed sees a full-screen "Verify your email" notice showing their address. | FLAG: dead end. The screen has no resend button and no sign-out control, even though POST /api/auth/resend-verification exists; it is only wired on the login page's error state. A user stuck here has to know to log out and fail a login to reach the resend. | BLOCKED, and unreachable in production. Supabase email confirmation is disabled (see area A), so no account can be in the unconfirmed state this screen serves. |
| PortalGuard's artist-only subscription and review-status checks are skipped for customers (subscriptionChecked is set immediately), so customers never see the artist plan gate or review banners. | Yes | WORKS. No plan gate or review banner appeared on any customer-portal page. |
| Sidebar navigation with five entries: My Orders, Saved, Addresses, Messages, Settings; the active route is highlighted by exact pathname match. | Yes | WORKS. Sidebar renders My Orders (/customer-portal), Saved, Addresses, Messages, Settings, with the active route highlighted. |
| Secondary sidebar links: Browse Art (to /browse) and a Logout button; Logout calls supabase.auth.signOut() and PortalGuard's effect then bounces the now-signed-out user to /login. | Yes | WORKS. Browse Art links /browse and Logout signs out and lands on /login; reloading a portal URL afterwards redirects to /login. |
| Sidebar footer profile chip: first letter of displayName in an avatar circle, the display name, and a fixed "Customer" role label. | Yes | WORKS. The chip renders "Q", "QA-TEST Customer" and the fixed label "Customer". |
| Mobile: hamburger button in a sticky "My Account" bar toggles the sidebar; a dark overlay closes it; every nav link closes it on tap. | Yes | WORKS. At 390x844 the sticky bar and hamburger render and the overlay menu opens with every portal link. |

## My Orders dashboard (/customer-portal) [registered customer]

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Page wraps its content in Suspense with a "Loading orders..." fallback (Next 16 useSearchParams requirement). | Yes | WORKS. The dashboard rendered its loading state before the orders fetch settled. |
| On mount, GET /api/orders loads the customer's orders; the server scopes the query to buyer_user_id = session user OR buyer_email = session email (email sanitised before the PostgREST .or() filter), so a customer only ever sees their own orders. | Yes | WORKS. GET /api/orders returned only this customer's order. Note the order row has buyer_user_id NULL, so the email branch of the .or() is what matched. |
| Order fetch failure handling: the catch is empty, loading simply ends. | FLAG: a failed /api/orders request renders the "No orders yet" empty state, which tells a customer with real orders that they have none. A distinct error state with retry is needed. | BLOCKED. No fetch failure was induced. |
| Stat card "Total Orders" shows orders.length. | Yes | WORKS. TOTAL ORDERS reads 1 against one order. |
| Stat card "Total Spent" sums o.total across every order and renders with a hard-coded pound sign. | FLAG: the sum includes cancelled and refunded orders, so it overstates actual spend, and it ignores the per-order currency field that the detail view respects via formatCurrency, so mixed-currency histories are summed as if all GBP. | WORKS as arithmetic for this account (TOTAL SPENT £53.49 against one £53.49 order). The flagged behaviours could not be exercised: the account has no cancelled, refunded or non-GBP order to reveal them. |
| Status filter pills (All, Active, Delivered, Cancelled) rendered as an aria tablist; Active excludes delivered and terminal states; Cancelled matches cancelled, refunded and disputed. | Yes, though the "Cancelled" pill silently bundling refunded and disputed under one label is slightly loose | WORKS. All four pills render as a tablist. |
| Date range filters From/To; the To bound is made inclusive to end of day so an order placed at 23:59 on the To date still matches. | Yes | BLOCKED. Only one order exists, so no date boundary case could be produced. |
| Free-text search across order_number, order id and item titles, case-insensitive. | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised meaningfully against a single order. |
| A "Clear" control appears only when any filter is active and resets status, dates and query. | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| Selected order, status filter, dates and query all persist in the URL (?order, ?status, ?from, ?to, ?q) via useUrlState, so filtered views and open orders survive reload and can be shared. | Yes | WORKS for the order param: opening the order rewrote the URL to /customer-portal?order=WS-J6CRQS4XTX2DJRO7 and the overlay survived that URL on reload. |
| Order list rows are full-width buttons that toggle the detail overlay; each shows order number (falling back to id), date, item count, total via formatCurrency, and a compact status pip tracker. | Yes | WORKS. The row is a full-width button showing WP-B73075, "30 Aug 2026 · 1 item", £53.49 and the status pip "Order placed". |
| "No orders match these filters." message when filters exclude everything. | Yes | BLOCKED. Not produced. |
| Empty state (no orders at all) with "Discover art" CTA to /browse. | Yes | BLOCKED. The account has an order, so the empty state does not render. |
| Detail overlay Close button clears the ?order URL param. | Yes | WORKS. Close is present in the overlay header. |
| Full OrderStatusTracker in the overlay: six pipeline steps with timestamps read from status_history; cancelled/refunded/disputed render as off-pipeline badges (red for cancelled, amber otherwise). | Yes | WORKS. Six steps render (Order placed, Artist notified, Awaiting dispatch, Processing, Shipped, Delivered) with the first stamped "30 Aug, 23:19". DIFFERS on one point: an open dispute does NOT show here. orders.status stayed "confirmed" after POST /api/disputes, so no off-pipeline disputed badge renders, while /orders/<id> for the same order shows "Problem reported". |
| Tracking number renders as an outbound carrier link when the format matches UPS, Royal Mail, FedEx or DHL patterns (detectCarrierUrl), otherwise as plain text. | Yes, sensible best-effort with a safe fallback | BLOCKED. The order carries no tracking number. |
| Items list with quantity and per-line totals, then Subtotal, Shipping (showing "Included" when 0), VAT (only when > 0) and Total, all currency-aware. | Yes | BROKEN on the per-line total. The overlay renders "Giraffe at Sunset × / £0.00" while orders.items stores lineTotal {"amount":4999,"currency":"GBP"} for that line. Subtotal £49.99, Shipping £3.50 and Total £53.49 below it are all correct, so only the item line is misread. The same defect appears on /orders/track. |
| Shipping address block (name, line 1, city, postcode) from the order's shipping JSON; legacy string-encoded items/status_history/shipping are defensively re-parsed on both server and client. | Yes | WORKS. "SHIPPING TO / QA-TEST Customer / 16 Ormond Crescent, Hampton TW12 2TH" renders from the order's shipping JSON. |
| Confirm delivery: shown only when status is "shipped", explains that confirming releases payment to the artist, disables while in flight, surfaces server errors inline, and optimistically flips the local status on success. Server-side, only the buyer may set "delivered" (BUYER_STATUSES in /api/orders PATCH), matched by buyer_user_id or buyer_email, and unconfirmed orders still pay out via the 14-day cron. | Yes, correct money-boundary design and honest copy | WORKS. The control appeared only at status shipped, carried the copy "Has this arrived? Confirming releases payment to the artist.", and disappeared once delivered. ||
| Deep links into the orders area from the rest of the platform: refund and order-status emails use SITE/customer-portal/orders, the refund-approved bell notification links /customer-portal/orders?id={id}, and /orders/[id] links /customer-portal/orders. | FLAG: /customer-portal/orders does not exist as a route and there is no redirect for it in next.config, so every one of those CTAs 404s. Orders live at /customer-portal and the detail param is ?order=, not ?id=. Senders live in other areas but the missing destination (or redirect) belongs here. | FIXED. /customer-portal/orders now 307s to /customer-portal, and /customer-portal/orders?id=WS-J6CRQS4XTX2DJRO7 307s to /customer-portal?order=WS-J6CRQS4XTX2DJRO7, translating the parameter as well as the path. Every one of those email and notification CTAs now lands correctly. |
| /api/orders GET returns the raw order rows (select *) to the buyer, including platform_fee, platform_fee_percent and stripe_payment_intent_id. | Yes, but note the platform's commission on the order is exposed to the buyer in the payload; harmless today, worth trimming to a buyer view model | WORKS, and the note holds. The buyer's own GET /api/orders payload includes platform_fee, platform_fee_percent and stripe_payment_intent_id (confirmed against the row: fee 2.50 at 5%, pi_3UAG8jFP3rMcNTgS0rM56UVd). |

| Cancel button closes the form and resets type, amount and reason. | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |

## Address book (/customer-portal/addresses + /api/customer-addresses) [registered customer]

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| List loads via GET /api/customer-addresses, ordered default-first then newest; a load failure shows an error toast. | Yes | WORKS. The list loads and the created address appears immediately with a DEFAULT badge. |
| API ownership: every read and write filters by the authenticated user_id on the service-role client, with table RLS as backstop, so one buyer can never read or mutate another's addresses even by guessing UUIDs. | Yes | MOSTLY WORKS, with one reporting defect. Ownership is genuinely enforced: GET returns only the caller's row, and PATCH on another user's address id returns 404 "Address not found" and changes nothing. But DELETE on that same foreign id returns 200 {"success":true} while deleting nothing (the other user's row is intact, verified in the database). No data is at risk, but DELETE reports success for a row it did not touch, inconsistent with PATCH's 404. |
| "Add address" button opens the create form and hides itself while any form is open. | Yes | WORKS. The header button opens the form; the empty-state link does not (see the dead-control row). |
| Form fields: full name, country (ISO select defaulting to GB), line 1, optional line 2, city, postcode; submit disabled until name, line 1, city, postcode and country are non-empty; Enter submits via the form's onSubmit. | Yes | WORKS. Fields render as Full name, Country (defaulting to United Kingdom), Address line 1, Address line 2 (optional), City, Postcode, plus a "Set as default" checkbox, and the submit stays disabled until the required fields are filled. |
| Server validation: zod schema with length caps plus a per-country postcode format check; field errors come back in issues.fieldErrors and the client surfaces the first one in an error toast. | Yes | WORKS. POST with postcode NOTAPOSTCODE returns 400 {"error":"Invalid address","issues":{"fieldErrors":{"postcode":["Postcode doesn't match the expected format for this country."]}}}, and omitting city returns the equivalent zod error for city. |
| Create: POST /api/customer-addresses; the first address ever saved is automatically made default server-side, and asking for default demotes the previous default first (respects the partial unique index). | Yes | WORKS. POST returned 201 and the address was made DEFAULT even though the client sent isDefault:false, which is the first-address-wins server rule. |

The dialog reads "Delete this address? / QA-TEST Customer, 16 Ormond Crescent, TW12 2TH" with Cancel and Delete; I cancelled and the row survived. The default re-election could not be exercised with one address. |

## Account deletion (AccountDangerZone + POST /api/account/delete + DELETE /api/account) [registered customer, shared with artist/venue]

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Type-to-confirm input requiring the exact string "DELETE MY ACCOUNT", with an aria-label, error reset on typing, and the red button disabled until the string matches. | Yes | WORKS. The input carries aria-label "Type DELETE MY ACCOUNT to confirm account deletion"; the red button is disabled when empty and with "delete my account" in the wrong case, and enables only on the exact string. I cleared the field without clicking, per the no-account-deletion rule. |
| Warning copy: "permanently deletes your profile, messages, saved items, and any orders attached to your account. This cannot be undone." | Yes, it accurately describes what POST /api/account/delete does | WORKS. The copy renders exactly as quoted. |
| POST /api/account/delete derives the user solely from the verified bearer token; a smuggled body user_id cannot delete someone else's account; the confirm string doubles as a CSRF-fluke seatbelt. | Yes | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Safety rule 2 forbids deleting an account; the three logins and the QA customer are all needed for the rest of the pass. Verified only that the button arms on the exact confirm string. |
| Erasure sweep: 40+ user_id-keyed tables deleted in FK-safe order, then auth.admin.deleteUser; customer_addresses and similar cascade via their FK to auth.users. | Yes for the user_id-keyed universe | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Same rule. |
| Failure policy on the hard delete: per-table errors are console.error'd and the sweep continues, then the auth user is deleted regardless. | FLAG: if a scrub fails, PII survives while the account is destroyed, so the person can no longer log in to notice, the exact failure mode the sibling soft-delete route documents and refuses (it returns 500 and keeps the account when any step fails). The two endpoints take opposite positions; the hard path should at least refuse auth deletion on scrub failure too. | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Same rule. |
| Legal posture of deleting orders: the hard delete removes orders and refund_requests rows outright, while DELETE /api/account exists precisely because those records "must be preserved for tax/legal reasons" and anonymises them instead. | FLAG: the customer-facing UI only offers the hard path, so every self-service deletion also destroys the platform's tax and dispute records. The two endpoints assert contradictory legal requirements; needs an owner ruling on which is correct, and the UI pointed at the survivor. | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Same rule; the failure policy cannot be observed without running a deletion. |
| Email-keyed residue after hard delete: orders matched only by buyer_email (guest and legacy purchases; per the orders route's own comment all live production orders are email-keyed with no buyer_user_id), plus newsletter_subscribers, email_suppressions and null-user email_events, are untouched. | FLAG: the route documents the newsletter/suppression gap as by-design, but email-keyed orders holding the buyer's name and shipping address are not in that documented list, and the soft-delete scrubs exactly those by email. A right-to-erasure "success" can leave the customer's address sitting in an order row. | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Same rule. The UI does still offer only the hard path: the settings danger zone is the sole deletion control and /account/delete 404s. |
| Demo protection: DELETE /api/account calls assertNotDemo; POST /api/account/delete does not. | FLAG: inconsistent. A configured demo account can hard-delete itself through the settings page while every other mutation in this area is demo-guarded. | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Same rule. Worth recording that the residue concern is live in shape: the order created this pass has buyer_user_id NULL and is matched only by buyer_email, exactly the class the flag says survives a hard delete. |
| On success: best-effort supabase signOut (may 401 since the auth user is gone, swallowed by design) then redirect to the homepage. | Yes, though there is no goodbye confirmation screen or deletion-confirmation email; acceptable for MVP, worth noting | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Same rule, and no demo account exists in production anyway. |
| Error display: ApiError code or "Could not delete your account." / network fallback, and the busy state releases. | Yes | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Same rule. |
| DELETE /api/account (soft delete, confirm "DELETE"): anonymises artist/venue profiles, deletes saved items and notifications, redacts sent messages, anonymises email-keyed orders/applications, deletes waitlist signups, checks every step, and refuses to delete the auth user if anything failed, telling the user support will finish by hand. | FLAG: sound endpoint, but nothing in the UI calls it, so it is dead from the product's perspective and users are never offered the anonymise-but-keep-history option; also the task brief expects GET /api/account, which does not exist (nothing calls one either). | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Same rule. |

## One-click unsubscribe (/account/email/unsubscribe page + /api/account/email/unsubscribe) [email recipient, no session]

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The page is a server component that applies the unsubscribe during GET render: validates ?c= against the category list, resolves the preference key, and upserts email_preferences.{key}=false for ?u= via the admin client, then renders the outcome. Treating the emailed link as the bearer is a deliberate, documented trust decision. | Yes as a model, with the flags below | BLOCKED. Not produced from a valid emailed link; the success copy was not rendered. |
| Success state: "You've been unsubscribed from {category}... Critical messages ... will still come through." | Yes | FLAG STANDS on the page side. /account/email/unsubscribe?c=newsletter (the footer link shape, no u) renders "We couldn't read the unsubscribe details from the link. Please use the link in the email, or sign in to update your preferences directly." Whether the email footer still omits u could not be checked, as /email-preview is 404 in production. |
| The visible footer unsubscribe link that EmailShell builds is /account/email/unsubscribe?c={category} with no u param. | FLAG: the page requires both c and u, so the link every notify/news email shows its reader always lands on the "couldn't read the unsubscribe details" failure state. Only the hidden List-Unsubscribe header URL carries u. The page's primary human entry point cannot ever succeed as wired. | FIXED as far as the transport goes. POST to the page URL returns 200 with the rendered page, not a 405, so a mail client's one-click POST no longer fails. Note the API route it should arguably point at also works for both verbs: GET and POST /api/account/email/unsubscribe both return 200 {"ok":true,...}. |
| RFC 8058 one-click: send.ts sets List-Unsubscribe-Post: List-Unsubscribe=One-Click, but the List-Unsubscribe URL is the PAGE (/account/email/unsubscribe), not the API. | FLAG: mail clients POST to the page URL, and App Router pages do not accept POST, so Gmail/Yahoo one-click unsubscribes fail with a 405. Meanwhile POST/GET /api/account/email/unsubscribe, built exactly for this, has zero references anywhere: dead code. Point the header at the API route. | FLAG STANDS, proven live and safely. An entirely unauthenticated GET /api/account/email/unsubscribe?c=newsletter&u=4ea8c2e2-… (the QA customer's raw UUID, no signature, no session) returned 200 and flipped that account's newsletter_enabled to false, creating the email_preferences row at 21:52:23. I restored it afterwards through the authenticated hub. Any category can be turned off for anyone whose UUID is known. |
| The u parameter is a raw user UUID with no signature or expiry. | FLAG: anyone who learns a user's UUID can silently flip their categories off, and link-scanning middleboxes that prefetch the GET will unsubscribe the user without a click. A signed token (HMAC of user+category) is the standard fix. | MOSTLY FIXED, and the residue is smaller than I first said. The same call with u=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000 returns 200 but inserts nothing: the route calls auth.admin.getUserById first and only upserts for a real user. Returning the identical message either way is a deliberate anti-enumeration choice documented in the route, not a defect. What remains is the unsigned u itself (next row) and the absence of a rate limit. |
| email_preferences.user_id has no FK to auth.users, and neither the page nor the API is rate limited. | FLAG: the unauthenticated upsert accepts arbitrary UUIDs, so junk rows can be inserted without bound. Add validation that the user exists (or the signed token above) plus a rate limit. | PARTLY FIXED. /account/delete still 404s, but no link to it renders on the email-preferences page any more (the page contains no internal anchors at all in the signed-in state). |
| Critical-category state ("Security alerts are required for service...") links "delete your account" to /account/delete. | FLAG: no such page exists, the link 404s. The deletion UI lives inside each portal's settings page; link there or to a real landing page. | WORKS. The missing-parameter state renders with the support routing. |
| Missing-params state and DB-error state with a support mailto. | Yes | BLOCKED. The comment cannot be read from production; behaviourally the API returns bare JSON for both verbs, which matches the flag's description of the actual behaviour. |
| API GET handler's comment says it redirects to the page-side success view; it actually returns bare JSON. | FLAG: cosmetic today because nothing links the API, but the comment misdescribes the behaviour; if the one-click fix lands, the GET should redirect as the comment intends. | BLOCKED. The comment cannot be read from production; behaviourally the API returns bare JSON for both GET and POST, which matches the flag's description of the actual behaviour. |

Still absent from the dump: purchase_offers, commissions and the visualizer tables. Also note termsAcceptances came back empty for an account that accepted the ToS at signup. |
| GET /api/account/export composes a JSON dump of profiles, works, placements, records, photos, messages both directions, orders as buyer (by email) and artist, refund requests, saved items, terms acceptances, notifications, applications, waitlist, enquiries and collections, served as an attachment. | FLAG: materially incomplete as a right-of-access response. It queries "applications" and "waitlist", tables that do not exist (real names artist_applications and waitlist_signups, per the erasure route's own verified audit), and keys artist_collections by user id where the column holds artist_profiles.id, so those sections are silently always empty (fetchAll swallows errors). It also omits customer_addresses, customer_profiles, email_preferences, purchase_offers, commissions and the visualizer tables that the deletion sweep demonstrably knows exist. And it is unthrottled despite fanning out 20+ admin queries per hit. | MOSTLY FIXED. GET /api/account/export returns 200, application/json, Content-Disposition attachment. The sections are correctly named and much wider than the flag describes: artistProfile, venueProfile, customerProfile, artistWorks, placements, placementRecords, placementPhotos, messages, orders, refundRequests, savedItems, termsAcceptances, notifications, artistApplications, waitlistSignups, enquiries, collections, customerAddresses, emailPreferences. The phantom "applications"/"waitlist" names and the omitted customer_addresses, customer_profiles and email_preferences are all fixed, and it IS throttled now (a third call in quick succession returned 429). Still absent: purchase_offers, commissions and the visualizer tables. Also note termsAcceptances came back empty for an account that accepted the ToS at signup. |

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Static, noindexed explainer reachable from the moderation emails' "Appeal this decision" CTA (OperationalPolicyViolationWarning, OperationalAccountRestricted both link it). | Yes | WORKS. /account/appeal renders the explainer with <meta name="robots" content="noindex, nofollow">. |
| Instructions to email appeals@wallplace.co.uk with account email, case reference, explanation and evidence. | Yes, appeal-by-email is a fair MVP scope, and it works for suspended users who cannot sign in; whether the mailbox exists is an ops question outside code | WORKS. The instructions name appeals@wallplace.co.uk and list the account email, case reference, explanation and supporting evidence. |
| Expectations list: acknowledgement within 2 business days, decision within 10, review by someone other than the original decision-maker. | Yes, provided operations can honour the stated SLAs | WORKS. POST /api/terms/accept from the customer signup returned 200 {"success":true} with the body {userEmail, userType:"customer", termsVersion:"v1.0-2026-04", termsType:"platform_tos"}. |
| Cross-link to the Complaints Policy at /complaints (page exists). | Yes | FLAG STANDS in shape. The customer signup fired terms/accept before any confirmation step, and because production has email confirmation disabled there is no later moment at which the acceptance could be re-stamped from a verified token. |

## Terms acceptance (/api/terms/accept, re-acceptance UI) [signing-up or signed-in user]

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| POST /api/terms/accept records an acceptance row: rate limited 10/min, zod-validated body, authenticated calls take the email from the token (the body cannot name a third party), audit IP only from platform headers (null over "unknown"), user agent recorded. | Yes | WORKS. POST /api/terms/accept from the customer signup returned 200 {"success":true} for the body {userEmail, userType:"customer", termsVersion:"v1.0-2026-04", termsType:"platform_tos"}. |
| Pre-signup path: the six signup/application callers fire before email confirmation, so unauthenticated acceptance rows keyed only by a body email remain forgeable. | FLAG: known, honestly documented residual; the sound fix (record acceptance post-confirmation from the token) is an owner decision on when the evidence is stamped. Carry it on the launch list rather than treating it as closed. | BLOCKED. The endpoint was not called directly; nothing in the customer surfaces exposed a plan state to check it against. |
| Terms re-acceptance UI: none exists. No component checks the accepted terms_version against a current version, and /api/terms/accept is only ever called from signup flows. | FLAG: when the terms are next revised there is no mechanism to prompt existing users to re-accept, so the acceptance trail will only ever hold each user's signup-era version. Fine for launch day one, but the gap should be a recorded follow-up. | WORKS. POST /api/auth/resend-verification for an unknown address returns 200 with the single neutral acknowledgement. Note its only entry point remains the login error state, and with confirmation disabled in production it can never be reached by a real user. |

## Supporting endpoints without their own customer UI [cross-checks]

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| GET /api/me/subscription returns { active, plan, userType, gatingEnabled } from the token; for customers it is always inactive with null plan (only artist/venue subscriptions exist), which callers of use-subscription handle. | Yes | BLOCKED. The endpoint was not called directly, and no customer surface exposes a plan state to check it against. |
| POST /api/auth/resend-verification: rate limited 3 per 5 minutes, byte-identical acknowledgement regardless of account state (no enumeration oracle), redirect built server-side through safeRedirect, anon client by design. | Yes, well built; its only UI entry is the login error state (see the PortalGuard flag for the missing portal-side entry point) | WORKS. POST /api/auth/resend-verification for an unknown address returns 200 with the single neutral acknowledgement. Note its only entry point remains the login error state, and with email confirmation disabled in production no real user can reach it. |
| /api/account exposes DELETE only; there is no GET "who am I" handler and nothing in the codebase calls one (profile data comes from the auth session and profile endpoints). | Yes, noting the audit brief expected a GET that does not exist | WORKS. Nothing in the customer portal calls a GET /api/account, and none was needed; profile data comes from the session and the profile endpoints. |

---

# D. Artist portal (Artist)

Scope: every page under `/artist-portal`, the components they mount (ArtistPortalLayout, PortalGuard, ApplicationGate, BlogEditor, ArtistPricingCards, PlacementActionItems, PaidLoanPaymentChip, OffersList, InstagramPostGenerator, PayoutExplainerModal, AccountDangerZone), and the backing APIs (`/api/artist-profile`, `/api/artist-works`, `/api/collections`, `/api/blogs*`, `/api/analytics/artist`, `/api/subscribe*`, `/api/me/subscription`, `/api/stripe-connect/*`, `/api/dashboard`, `/api/saved`, `/api/orders`, `/api/refunds*`, `/api/works/[id]/mockups`, `/api/qr/[slug]`, `/api/offers*`, `/api/artwork-requests/[id]/responses`). The shared inbox component internals, the shared placement detail page and the checkout side are other agents' areas. "Demo guard" below refers to `assertNotDemo` (soft: returns 200 with `{demo:true}` and no real write) and `assertNotDemoStrict` (403), active only when `DEMO_ARTIST_USER_ID` / `DEMO_VENUE_USER_ID` env vars are set.

## Portal access, guards and chrome (/artist-portal/* via layout.tsx, PortalGuard, ArtistPortalLayout)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| Every `/artist-portal/*` route is wrapped in `PortalGuard allowedType="artist"`: signed-out users are `router.replace`d to `/login` | Yes | WORKS. Signing out and reloading an /artist-portal route redirects to /login (verified in area A). |
| Signed-in user of a different role (venue, customer, admin) gets an info toast "This is the artist portal. Redirecting to your X portal." and is redirected to their own portal via `portalPathForRole` | Yes | BLOCKED. The customer and venue accounts were tested in their own portals rather than pointed at this one. |
| Unverified email (no `email_confirmed_at`) renders a "Verify your email" full-page notice with the user's address instead of the portal | Yes | BLOCKED, and unreachable in production: Supabase email confirmation is disabled, so no account can be unverified. |
| PortalGuard fetches `/api/artist-profile` on every portal page to read `review_status` and `subscription_status`; billing and settings are exempt from the subscription check so lapsed users can fix billing | Yes | WORKS. GET /api/artist-profile fires on every portal page load; billing and settings both loaded normally. |
| `review_status = pending`: portal stays usable with a full-width amber "Your application is under review" banner (offset `lg:ml-56` to clear the fixed sidebar) plus a "Build profile" link | Yes | BLOCKED. The artist is approved; no pending account was available. |
| `review_status = rejected`: portal replaced by "Application not approved" dead-end with a mailto link to applications@wallplace.art | Yes | BLOCKED. No rejected account was available. |
| `review_status = approved` with `subscription_status` none or incomplete: portal usable with a persistent "You're approved. Pick a plan..." banner linking to billing; copy promises "Your first month is free" | Yes, matches the 30-day trial for first-time subscribers in `/api/subscribe` | BLOCKED. The artist has an active Pro subscription, so the approved-no-plan banner never rendered. |
| `subscription_status` past_due or canceled: portal (except billing/settings) replaced by a "Choose Your Plan" block with "Choose a Plan" (to billing) and "Compare Plans" (to /pricing) buttons | Yes, though see the trial-copy flag on this block below | BLOCKED. The artist is active, not past_due or canceled. |
| The past_due and canceled gate copy says "All plans include a free trial, and you won't be charged until it ends. All plans include a first month free." | FLAG: this block is only ever shown to past_due and canceled users, and `/api/subscribe` gives `hadPreviousSub` users zero trial days, so the exact audience seeing this promise is the one audience that will be charged immediately | BLOCKED. The gate block only renders for past_due/canceled, which could not be produced without cancelling a real subscription. |
| On a profile-fetch error PortalGuard fails open (lets the user through) | Yes, reasonable for a read guard | BLOCKED. No fetch failure was induced. |
| ArtistPortalLayout independently re-checks auth and redirects non-artists to `/login`, and checks `artist_profiles` row existence; accounts with no profile row are redirected to `/apply` so they complete the application first | Yes | WORKS. Every /artist-portal route loaded straight into the portal for the artist account. |
| Full-screen animated "Loading your portal..." bar shown until both the auth check and the profile-existence check settle, preventing a chrome flash before the /apply redirect | Yes | WORKS. The animated loading bar renders before the portal chrome on each first load. |
| A failed profile-existence fetch (network error) also redirects to `/apply` | Yes with a caveat: a transient network blip on an existing artist bounces them to the application page, but the page itself lets them return | BLOCKED. Not induced. |
| Sidebar nav: Dashboard, Edit Profile, My Portfolio, Showroom, Messages, Placements, My Offers, Artwork Requests, Collections, Saved, Orders, QR Labels, Social Posts, (Blogs when BLOGS_V1 on), Analytics, Billing; Settings and Logout in a secondary section under a divider | Yes | DIFFERS. The production sidebar is Dashboard, Edit Profile, My Portfolio, Messages, ENQUIRIES, Placements, My Offers, Collections, Saved, Orders, QR Labels, Social Posts, Blogs, Analytics, Billing, then Settings and Logout under a divider. Showroom and Artwork Requests are both absent (parked), and Enquiries is a new entry the audit does not list. |
| Blogs nav entry only rendered when `isFlagOn("BLOGS_V1")` (prodDefault false), so production hides an editor whose API would 403 every save | Yes, gate and API agree | DIFFERS. Blogs IS in the sidebar and /artist-portal/blogs, /blogs/new and /blogs/[id]/edit all render and save in production, so BLOGS_V1 is ON, not off. |
| Active nav item highlighted by exact `activePath` match; document.title synced to "Section \| Artist Portal \| Wallplace" including sub-route prefix matching | Yes |  WORKS. The active item is highlighted and document.title reads "My Portfolio \| Artist Portal \| Wallplace", "Analytics \| Artist Portal \| Wallplace", "Billing \| Artist Portal \| Wallplace" and so on. |
| Mobile: hamburger top bar toggles the sidebar as an overlay; tapping the dark backdrop or any nav item closes it | Yes | WORKS. Verified at 390x844 in area A: the overlay opens and closes on backdrop or link tap. |
| Sidebar footer shows profile image (or initial avatar) plus display name and the static role label "Artist" | Yes | WORKS. The footer shows the profile image, "Fin Coles" and the static label "Artist". |
| Logout button calls `signOut()` from AuthContext | Yes | WORKS. Logout signs out and lands on /login; a portal reload afterwards redirects to /login. |
| ApplicationGate (used by /apply, the portal's feeder): loading state, signed-out replace-redirect to `/signup/artist?next=/apply`, wrong-user-type notice with "Create artist account" CTA, artists (or legacy accounts without user_type) see the ApplicationForm | Yes | WORKS. Verified in area A, including the wrong-role notice whose button genuinely signs the user out. |
| `POST /api/artist-profile` (application/claim flow) creates the profile with `review_status: "pending"` server-side; slug and review_status are server-owned so a client cannot self-approve | Yes | BLOCKED. Creating a fresh artist profile would mean a new account; not run. |

The inputs to it are verified in the next row. |
| Venue attribution on labels printed from this page | FLAG: LabelSheet supports a `venueSlug` field that becomes the `vs=` param, and `/api/qr` only resolves venue_user_id (analytics) and the signed `va` attribution token (checkout revenue share) from `vs`; but this page's `buildLabels` only ever sets `venueName` (the display name from the placements list) and never `venueSlug`. So labels printed here carry `v=` only: the scan is logged without a venue_user_id, the redirect carries no `venue` slug or signed claim, and the venue's QR-sale revenue share cannot attribute, precisely the failure mode the qr route's own comment says name-only caused. The placements deep link also passes the venue NAME. The page needs to carry the placement's `venue_slug` through to the labels | FIXED. Reading the label component's React props in the live preview with Testing Venue selected returns BOTH venueName="Testing Venue" AND venueSlug="testing-venue". The slug IS carried through now, so the printed QR gets vs=, which is what resolves venue_user_id and mints the signed attribution token. |
| Legacy printed labels with `work=`/`v=` params still resolve via the API's fallbacks | Yes | WORKS. Verified in area A: /api/qr/fin-coles?work=…&v=… still resolves via the legacy fallbacks. |

| Status actions: confirmed -> "Mark as Processing", processing -> "Mark as Shipped" (with tracking input); PATCH `/api/orders` validates the transition, appends status_history, and surfaces API errors inline | Yes | WORKS as affordances: the confirmed order shows "Mark as Processing". Not pressed, to avoid moving a real order's state. |
| No "Mark as Delivered" action for the seller | Yes, deliberate: delivery confirmation releases the 14-day escrow, the API 403s seller attempts (`SELLER_STATUSES`), the buyer confirms from the customer portal, and unconfirmed orders pay out on the 14-day cron; removing the button rather than disabling it is the right call | WORKS by absence: no "Mark as Delivered" action renders anywhere on the seller side. |
| Tracking-number helper text: "Required for £100+ orders (signed-for delivery)." | FLAG: nothing enforces this. The input is optional client-side and `PATCH /api/orders` accepts shipped with no trackingNumber at any order value; either enforce the rule for £100+ orders or soften the copy to a recommendation | WORKS as copy. The helper text renders under the tracking field. The rule itself could not be exercised: this order was £53.49, under the £100 threshold, and the field was optional. ||
| Emails on transitions: `recordOrderEvent` dispatches the buyer's processing/shipped/delivered/cancelled templates (idempotency-keyed) with the artist copied on order-placed; a separate inline send covers disputed/refunded only, so no duplicate sends; delivered also stamps delivered_at once and triggers early payout release of pending transfers | Yes | DIFFERS. The buyer's processing, shipped and delivered templates all dispatched with the right subjects. "and the artist" does not happen, see row 874. ||
| Items list reads both legacy (`qty`/`price`) and enriched (`quantity`/`lineTotal` pence) shapes so neither generation renders £NaN | Yes | WORKS. The new order's item renders "Giraffe at Sunset × 1 / £49.99" from the enriched lineTotal pence shape, with no £NaN. Worth contrasting: the CUSTOMER portal and /orders/track render the same line as £0.00. |
| Revenue breakdown: items subtotal, shipping line, sale total, venue share %, platform fee %, "Your revenue", plus the footnote that the fee % reflects the plan at time of sale | Yes | WORKS. The breakdown reads Items subtotal £49.99, Shipping +£3.50, Sale total £53.49, Platform fee (5%) -£2.50, Your revenue £50.99, with the plan-at-time-of-sale footnote. Every figure reconciles with the orders row. |
| Collection orders (`fulfilment_method: "collection"`): buyer's proposed pickup window and contact details shown prominently with a prefilled "Email buyer to confirm pickup" mailto | Yes | BLOCKED. No collection-fulfilment order exists. |
| Ship orders show the full shipping address block | Yes | WORKS. "SHIP TO / QA-TEST Customer / 16 Ormond Crescent / Hampton, TW12 2TH". |
| Refund requests for the order loaded from `GET /api/refunds` (artist scope = requests on their orders); pending ones render in an amber panel with requester type, reason, type and amount | Yes | WORKS. A REFUND PENDING badge renders on order WS-H7RgZntN in the list. |
| "Approve Refund" button (money handler): POSTs `/api/refunds/process` action approve. This is a deliberately gated money mover: the API allows only the order's artist or an admin, refuses artist self-approval of artist-raised requests, takes an atomic pending->processing claim so double-clicks cannot double-refund, executes the Stripe refund with pro-rated transfer reversals (shipping reversed against the artist leg only), and emails buyer and artist plus an in-app bell | Yes on the server; but see the silent-failure flag two rows down | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Safety rule 3 forbids approving a real refund; this moves money on a real record I did not create. |
| "Reject" button with a required-ish reason input (reason optional in the API): same owner-gated endpoint, records resolved_reason, emails the buyer a rejection | Yes, same gating | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Same rule. |
| Client feedback when processing fails: `processRefund` only updates state `if (res.ok)`; on a 403/409/500 nothing happens visually (no toast, no inline error) | FLAG: a rejected process call (for example a 409 "already processed", a 403 on an artist-raised request, or a demo-guard response) leaves the artist staring at an unchanged panel with zero explanation; this is the one money flow on the page still using bare authFetch with no error surface | BLOCKED, and NOT SAFE to force. Observing the silent-failure path requires firing a refund process call that gets rejected, which means touching the money endpoint on a real record. |
| "Issue Refund" (proactive, full or partial with amount cap at order total and a required reason): creates a request via `/api/refunds/request` (requester_type resolves to "artist") then immediately calls `/api/refunds/process` approve on it | FLAG: the second step is deliberately owner/admin-gated and will always 403 for the artist ("Artist-initiated refunds require admin approval", the intentional no-self-approval rule). So the button's real behaviour is "file a refund request for admin review", yet the UI closes the form as if done, adds nothing to the visible list on the 403, and never tells the artist their refund is now waiting on an admin. The gating is correct and deliberate; the button label and the silent aftermath are not. Rename the action and surface the queued state | NOT SAFE TO TEST. "Issue Refund" renders on the order detail; pressing it files a real refund request against a real order. |
| Empty state "No orders yet. When someone buys your work, orders will appear here." with a Discover art CTA | Yes; minor: pointing an artist at /browse to fix having no orders is an odd CTA | BLOCKED. The artist has orders, so the empty state does not render. |

---

# E. Venue portal and curation (Venue)

Scope: every page under /venue-portal, the shared portal shell, the wall visualiser end to end, the curation booking flow under /curated, and the backing APIs listed in the audit brief. Perspective is the signed-in venue user. Demo-account notes are called out where a guard changes behaviour. Column 3 is left empty for production testing.

## Portal shell and access control (/venue-portal/* via layout.tsx, PortalGuard, VenuePortalLayout)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| Route-group layout wraps every /venue-portal page in PortalGuard with allowedType="venue" | Yes | WORKS. Every /venue-portal route loaded straight into the portal for the venue account. |
| PortalGuard: signed-out user is replaced to /login | Yes | WORKS. Verified in area A: signing out and reloading a portal URL redirects to /login. |
| PortalGuard: signed-in user of another role (artist, customer, admin) sees an info toast ("This is the venue portal. Redirecting to your X portal.") and is redirected to their own portal | Yes | BLOCKED. Cross-role redirection was not driven; each account was tested in its own portal. |
| PortalGuard: user with unconfirmed email sees a full-page "Verify your email" block naming their address; no portal content renders | Yes | BLOCKED, and unreachable in production: Supabase email confirmation is disabled, so no account can be unconfirmed. |
| PortalGuard: artist-only subscription and review gates are skipped for venues (subscriptionChecked short-circuits to true) | Yes, venue path is clean | WORKS. No plan gate or review banner rendered on any venue page. |
| VenuePortalLayout sidebar nav: Dashboard, Venue Profile, Messages, Placements, My Offers, Artwork Requests, My Walls, Saved, QR Labels, Analytics, My Orders, plus Settings and Logout at the bottom | FLAG: /venue-portal/enquiries exists and is reachable from the dashboard Quick Action "View Enquiries", but has no sidebar entry, so the page is orphaned from the main nav. Either add it or remove the page (see Enquiries section, where the page is dead anyway) | DIFFERS. Production renders Dashboard, Venue Profile, Messages, Placements, My Offers, My Walls, Saved, QR Labels, Analytics, My Orders, then Settings and Logout. Artwork Requests is absent (parked). |
| Sidebar active-state highlighting uses longest-prefix matching so nested routes (e.g. /venue-portal/walls/abc) highlight the parent item | Yes | WORKS. Nested routes highlight the parent item. |
| Per-route document.title ("Placements · Venue Portal · Wallplace" etc.) set on navigation, longest-prefix matched | Yes | WORKS. Titles read "Dashboard · Venue Portal · Wallplace", "Placements · Venue Portal · Wallplace", "Settings · Venue Portal · Wallplace", "My Walls · Venue Portal · Wallplace". |
| Duplicate inline auth check in VenuePortalLayout replaces to /login when not a venue (belt and braces on top of PortalGuard) | Yes, harmless duplication | WORKS by implication: the portal loads for the venue and redirects everyone else. |
| Self-heal on portal load: PATCH /api/venue-profile {ensureProfile:true} creates or adopts the caller's venue_profiles row (adoption only via confirmed email, exactly one orphan; insert hydrates from venue_registrations; slug suffixing on collision) | Yes, and the E34 hardening (no browser-chosen slug adoption) is correct | WORKS. The portal loaded with the venue's real profile hydrated (name, type, location, wall space, footfall), so the ensureProfile self-heal resolved. |
| Self-heal only runs when email_confirmed_at is set; unconfirmed users get neither adoption nor hydration | Yes, deliberate | BLOCKED, and moot: confirmation is disabled in production so every account is confirmed. |
| Self-heal failure banner: "We couldn't finish setting up your venue portal..." with a Retry button that re-runs the PATCH | Yes | BLOCKED. No self-heal failure was induced. |
| Mobile: hamburger toggles a slide-in drawer copy of the nav; backdrop click closes; drawer is aria-hidden when closed | Yes | BLOCKED. The mobile drawer was not opened on the venue portal specifically; the equivalent overlay is verified on the artist and customer portals. |
| Loading state: animated progress bar with "Loading your portal..." | Yes | WORKS. The animated "Loading your portal..." bar renders before the chrome. |
| Logout button in sidebar calls signOut() | Yes | WORKS. Logout signs out and lands on /login. |

## Dashboard (/venue-portal)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| Header greeting "Welcome back, {displayName}" with fallback "there" | Yes | WORKS. "Welcome back, Test" renders. |
| Data load: parallel fetch of /api/dashboard, /api/placements and /api/analytics/venue?range=all on mount | Yes | WORKS. /api/dashboard, /api/placements and /api/analytics/venue all fire on mount. |
| Stats tile "Saved Artists": count of savedItems with type="artist" from SavedContext | Yes | WORKS. Saved Artists reads 5. |
| Stats tile "Total Spent": sum of order.total across ALL orders returned by /api/dashboard | FLAG: the venue branch of /api/dashboard returns orders matched by venue_slug OR buyer_email, so placement SALES made by customers at the venue are summed into "Total Spent" alongside the venue's own purchases. A venue with QR sales but no purchases sees a non-zero "spent" figure they never spent. Should sum only buyer_email-matched orders (as the Orders page does) | DIFFERS, and the conflation does not appear to occur. The tile reads Total Spent £609.75, and the Orders page separately reports "YOUR PURCHASES 4 / SPENT ON ART £609.75" against "PLACEMENT SALES 2 / REVENUE EARNED £10.00". The two figures are distinct, so the dashboard tile is showing purchases only. |
| Stats tile "Revenue Share Earned": sum of placement.revenue across /api/placements rows | Yes, placements.revenue is trigger-maintained on delivery per migration 038 | WORKS. Revenue Share Earned reads £10.00, matching the Orders page's REVENUE EARNED. |
| Stats tile "QR Scans": lifetime qr_scan count from /api/analytics/venue?range=all | Yes | WORKS. QR Scans reads 3, matching the analytics page's lifetime figure. |
| Loading state renders a pulsing dash in each tile | Yes | WORKS. Tiles render a pulsing dash before the fetches settle. |
| Getting Started checklist item "Complete your venue profile" (name + type + location present) linking to /venue-portal/profile | Yes | WORKS. Ticked green for this venue, linking /venue-portal/profile. |
| Checklist item "Describe what you're looking for" (any preferred_styles or preferred_themes) linking to /venue-portal/profile | Yes, but see the Profile flag about fabricated default styles: a venue who never chose styles can be marked complete after one Save | WORKS. Ticked green, linking /venue-portal/profile. |
| Checklist item "Browse artist portfolios", completed via localStorage flag set on click of the checklist link | Yes, though completion is per-browser (localStorage), so it resets on a new device | WORKS as designed. Rendered as an unticked circle linking /browse; completion is per-browser. |
| Checklist item "Send your first enquiry", completed when sentMessageCount > 0 | FLAG: sentMessageCount counts ANY message row where sender_name equals the venue slug, so a reply to an artist-initiated thread completes "Send your first enquiry"; also the link points to /browse, not the enquiry mechanism itself. Loose but arguably acceptable; the label overstates precision | BLOCKED for the flagged cause. The item IS ticked for this venue, but whether that came from a real enquiry or from any message row bearing the venue slug cannot be separated from outside. |
| Checklist item "Set up payouts", completed when profile.stripe_connect_account_id is truthy, linking to /venue-portal/settings | FLAG: completes as soon as a Stripe account row is CREATED, not when onboarding completes or payouts are enabled, so a venue who abandoned Stripe onboarding is shown a ticked "Set up payouts" while Settings still says "Continue Setup" | FIXED. This venue HAS a Stripe account id (settings shows the incomplete state, "Complete your payout setup to start receiving transfers" with a Continue Setup button) and the checklist item renders as an UNTICKED circle. The two surfaces agree now; the item no longer completes on a half-finished onboarding. |
| Checklist progress bar, n/N pill, all-complete celebration state, auto-dismiss (localStorage flag) 3s after all complete, and a manual Dismiss link | Yes | WORKS. The bar reads "3 of 5 complete" with a 3/5 pill and a Dismiss link. |
| Placement Action Items block (shared PlacementActionItems, role="venue"): surfaces pending requests the venue can respond to, plus schedule/install/live to-dos on active placements; renders nothing when empty | Yes | WORKS. "Action items 3" renders schedule-install rows, each linking /placements/<id>. |
| Wallplace Curated promo card "Want a shortlist picked for you? ... from £49" with Explore Curated button to /curated | Yes, price matches the tier table | WORKS. The card reads "Want a shortlist picked for you? Our curators hand-pick works that fit your space, from £49." with Explore Curated to /curated, and /curated's cheapest tier is indeed £49. |
| Quick Actions: Browse Portfolios (/browse), View Enquiries (/venue-portal/enquiries), Your Orders (/venue-portal/orders), Update Preferences (/venue-portal/profile) | FLAG: "View Enquiries" routes to a page whose backing API has no GET handler, so it always shows an empty list (see Enquiries section) | FIXED. Quick Actions are Browse Portfolios (/browse), "Your Messages" (/venue-portal/messages), Your Orders (/venue-portal/orders) and Update Preferences (/venue-portal/profile). The flagged link to the non-existent /venue-portal/enquiries is gone. |
| Summary card repeating saved artists, total spent, revenue share, QR scans, plus a Browse Art button | Yes, same caveat as the Total Spent tile | WORKS. The Summary card repeats all four figures with a Browse Art button. |
| Recent Activity feed: pending placement requests received (linking to /placements/[id]), accepted/declined outcomes on requests the venue sent, and orders placed (linking to /venue-portal/orders), sorted newest first, capped at 8 | Yes, the requester-direction filtering is correct (uses requester_user_id resolved by the API) | WORKS. The feed renders ORDER rows ("Order placed for £64.49, Fin Coles") and ACCEPTED rows ("Placement accepted: Mt. Fitz Roy, Fin Coles") newest first. |
| Activity empty state "No recent activity yet. Start by browsing portfolios." | Yes | BLOCKED. The venue has activity, so the empty state does not render. |
| Relative timestamps (just now / Nm ago / Nh ago / Nd ago / date) | Yes | WORKS. Rows read "2d ago" and dated forms like "15 Jun". |

| Duplicate bottom Save Changes button with "Make sure to save before leaving this page." hint | Yes | WORKS. A second Save Changes renders at the foot with "Make sure to save before leaving this page." |
| Unsaved-changes warning on tab close and on client-side navigation via useUnsavedWarning | Yes | WORKS. Navigating away from a dirty profile fired a beforeunload dialog. |
| Venue Details section with per-section Edit/Cancel toggle: Venue Name, Venue Type, Location, Wall Space, Visitors per day | Yes, read-only display falls back to "Not set" without leaking into the input (E42-a fix present) | WORKS. | Visitors-per-day input strips non-digits on paste/typing | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| Cleared fields save as NULL (payload sends null, not undefined) so a venue can genuinely blank a field | Yes (E42-d fix present) | BLOCKED. No field was cleared and saved. |
| Section "Cancel" link only exits edit mode; it does not revert typed values | FLAG: pressing Cancel on the Venue Details or Display Needs section keeps the edited values in state (and dirty), so "Cancel" reads like revert but only hides the inputs; the next Save still persists the "cancelled" edits | FIXED. Entering QA-TEST-CANCEL-PROBE into Venue Name and pressing Cancel restored the display to "Testing Venue". The edit is reverted, not kept. |
| Venue Photos gallery: up to 10 images, file-picker upload ("Add photos") and drag-and-drop with a highlighted drop target | Yes | WORKS. The gallery renders with an "Add photos" control and the cap explained as up to 10 images. |
| Photo uploads resize client-side (2400px, 0.92 WebP) via uploadImage into the "collections" storage bucket | Yes functionally, though parking venue photos in a bucket named "collections" is a naming smell | BLOCKED. No photo was uploaded. |
| Per-photo hover Remove button; cap message at 10 photos; upload errors shown inline | Yes | BLOCKED. No photo was uploaded or removed. |
| Art Preferences "Interested In" toggles: Revenue share (interested_in_revenue_share), Paid loan (stored as interested_in_free_loan), Direct purchase (interested_in_direct_purchase) | Yes, the "Paid loan" label over the legacy free_loan column matches the canonical K3 semantics, and all three are on the write allowlist. Note there is no longer any way to express interest in a genuinely free display | WORKS. INTERESTED IN renders Revenue share, Paid loan and Direct purchase as toggles. |
| interested_in_collections exists in the DB and is served by /api/venues/demand, but the profile page has no control for it | FLAG: the column defaults from registration and can never be changed in the portal; /spaces filters read it, so the venue cannot correct a wrong value | FLAG STANDS. There is no control for interested_in_collections anywhere on the page; the word "collection" does not appear on it at all. |
| Preferred Styles tag pills (10 options) and Preferred Themes tag pills (8 options), toggle to select, persisted as preferred_styles / preferred_themes | Yes, but see next row | WORKS. Ten style pills and eight theme pills render. |
| Empty-profile defaults: when the saved row has no styles/themes the page pre-selects Contemporary/Minimal/Photography and Nature/City/Architecture | FLAG: fabricated preferences. A venue that opens the page and hits Save (for any other edit) silently persists tags they never chose, and artists then target them on false data. Should start empty | BLOCKED. This venue has saved styles and themes, so the empty-profile default could not be observed. |
| "Prefer artists within 10 miles of my venue" toggle (interested_in_local_artists) | Yes, column exists since migration 103 and is on the allowlist, so the tick persists | WORKS. "ARTIST LOCATION / Prefer artists within 10 miles of my venue" renders as a real toggle. |
| Preferred Artwork Sizes pills (Small/Medium/Large/Oversized) | FLAG: decorative. The selection is not included in the save payload, there is no preferred_sizes column (confirmed vestigial in writable-fields.ts), and state resets to a hardcoded Medium+Large on every load. Remove the control or add the column | FIXED, by removal. There is no "Preferred Artwork Sizes" section on the page: no such heading and no Small/Medium/Large/Oversized pills. The decorative control is gone. |
| Display Needs section (Edit toggle): Wall Space Available, Lighting, Installation Notes, Rotation Frequency, each persisted to display_* columns | Yes | WORKS. The Display Needs section renders with its own Edit toggle and four fields. |
| Live preview panel mirroring the public venue card in real time (hero photo, photo count badge, arrangement chips, styles, display needs, themes), sticky on desktop | Yes, faithful to the /venues/[slug] card fields | WORKS. No demo account exists in production. |

## Settings (/venue-portal/settings)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| Account Details section gated behind the venue fetch so uncontrolled defaults are seeded from real data | Yes | WORKS. Account Details renders seeded from the real profile. |
| Venue Name, Email Address and Phone Number fields | FLAG: dead form. All three are uncontrolled inputs with no save button, no submit handler and no wiring to any API; anything typed is silently discarded on navigation. Either wire them (name to venue-profile, email to auth email-change, phone to venue-profile) or render read-only | FIXED. The form is no longer dead. Venue Name and Email Address are now read-only display values with explanatory notes ("Change your venue name on the Venue Profile page", "This is the address you sign in with"), and the editable fields are Contact Name, Phone Number, Address Line 1, Address Line 2, City and Postcode with a real "Save Details" button. |
| Password row links to /forgot-password ("Change password") | Yes, reasonable flow | WORKS. A "Change password" link renders under Password. |
| Notification Preferences: three checkboxes (Order updates, Message notifications, Wallplace news and digest) with "Changes save automatically", via useNotificationPrefs against /api/account/preferences | See flags below | DIFFERS. Only TWO toggles render now, "Message notifications" and "Wallplace news & digest", under "Changes save automatically." The Order updates checkbox is gone. |
| GET /api/account/preferences for a venue selects email_digest_enabled, message_notifications_enabled AND order_notifications_enabled from venue_profiles | FLAG: venue_profiles has no order_notifications_enabled column (migration 050 deliberately skipped it, and the live-schema snapshot confirms it), so the SELECT errors and the endpoint 500s for every venue. The hook swallows the failure, so the three checkboxes ALWAYS render the opt-in defaults regardless of what the venue previously saved | FIXED. GET /api/account/preferences as the venue returns 200 {"preferences":{"email_digest_enabled":true,"message_notifications_enabled":true}}. order_notifications_enabled is no longer selected, so the phantom column can no longer 500 the read. |
| Toggling "Order updates" PATCHes {order_notifications_enabled} to venue_profiles | FLAG: the UPDATE hits the same missing column, so it 500s every time; the checkbox reverts with "Could not save preference." There is no venue order-notification pref to save. Remove the row for venues or add the column (venues do buy art, so adding it is defensible) | FIXED, by removal on both sides. There is no Order updates checkbox, and PATCHing {"order_notifications_enabled":false} returns 400 {"error":"No valid preference fields supplied."} rather than 500. |
| Toggling "Message notifications" or "Wallplace news and digest" PATCHes single existing columns | Yes, the write itself succeeds, but because GET always fails the displayed initial state never reflects the stored value, so a venue who previously opted out sees the box ticked again on every visit | WORKS. With the GET now succeeding, the two remaining toggles render their real stored values. |
| "Wallplace news & digest" description says it controls "Platform announcements and feature launches" | FLAG: the weekly venue digest email is suppressed via the separate email_preferences.digests_enabled flag consulted by sendEmail, not venue_profiles.email_digest_enabled, so this checkbox does not actually stop the digest; the real control lives on the email-preferences page reached from email footers. Misleading copy plus a split preference store | FLAG STANDS. The description still reads "Platform announcements and feature launches" while the venue digest is controlled by the separate email_preferences hub at /account/email. |
| Payouts section, loading state, then one of three states from /api/stripe-connect/status: not started ("Set Up Payouts"), account exists but incomplete ("Continue Setup"), complete ("Payouts Active" pill + "Open Stripe Dashboard") | Yes | WORKS. The incomplete state renders: "Complete your payout setup to start receiving transfers." with a Continue Setup button. |
| "Set Up Payouts" / "Continue Setup" POST /api/stripe-connect/onboard with accountType:"venue" and redirect to the returned Stripe onboarding URL; refresh/return URLs land back on /venue-portal/settings | Yes, the onboard route supports the venue table and venue return URLs | BLOCKED. Not pressed; it redirects into Stripe onboarding. |
| "Open Stripe Dashboard" POST /api/stripe-connect/dashboard and redirect to the Express login link | Yes | BLOCKED. This venue's onboarding is incomplete, so the dashboard button does not render. |
| Payout errors surface via toast; buttons disable while redirecting | Yes, though the catch shows err.code (a machine slug) rather than err.message, so the toast can read like "stripe_error" instead of a sentence | BLOCKED. No payout error was induced. |
| PayoutExplainerModal fires once per user after onboarding completes (audience="venue" copy), dismissal persisted in localStorage | Yes | BLOCKED. Onboarding is incomplete, so the explainer has not fired for this venue. |
| AccountDangerZone: type "DELETE MY ACCOUNT" to enable the red button, POST /api/account/delete, then sign-out and redirect home | Yes, hard-delete confirm string gate is sound; error path also shows err.code rather than the message | WORKS up to the confirm gate, which is all safety rule 2 allows; the same component is fully exercised on the customer settings page. |

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|

| Archive semantics: hidden_for_venue column takes precedence, placement_archives table is fallback only when the column is absent | Yes | BLOCKED. Archive semantics were not exercised. |

Tabs render Current 11, Pending 1, Active 7, Completed 0, Archived 35. |

Rows render thumbnail, title with "+1 more" and "+5 more" chips on bundles, artist, and canonical arrangement chips (Paid loan, Revenue share, Paid loan + QR with the percentage). |
| Earned column only renders a figure when revenue_share_percent > 0, otherwise a dash | Yes | WORKS. Rows with a share show £0.00; rows without show a dash. |
| Status cell: Pending shows "Awaiting their reply" vs "Your turn" depending on direction; Declined/Cancelled fixed pills; Active/Completed/Sold render as an inline select letting the venue set Active/Completed/Sold directly | Yes, direction-aware copy is good; the raw status dropdown is blunt but the shared updatePlacementStatus rolls back on server rejection | WORKS. A sent pending row shows "Awaiting their reply"; Cancelled rows show a fixed pill; Active rows render the inline Active/Completed/Sold select. |
| Archived tab overrides every status badge with a single "Archived" pill | Yes | BLOCKED. The Archived tab was not opened. |
| MiniStatusBar 5-dot lifecycle indicator on Active/Completed/Sold rows | Yes | WORKS. Active rows render the lifecycle indicator. |

| Cancel button (amber) on Active/Pending rows opens a ConfirmDialog then PATCHes status=cancelled; optimistic with rollback and toast on failure | Yes | WORKS as an affordance: Cancel renders on Active and Pending rows. Not pressed, to avoid cancelling a real placement. |
| Archive/unarchive bin icon per row with confirm dialog; DELETE /api/placements?id=... (&unarchive=1), optimistic, 404 treated as already-done | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| Bulk select: header checkbox with indeterminate state, per-row checkboxes, action bar with Clear and Archive/Unarchive selected (looped DELETEs, full rollback if any fail) | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| Expanded row detail: artist, size, arrangement, revenue share, monthly fee, requested/responded dates, earned, QR scans | Mostly, but see the two flags below | WORKS. Expanding a row shows Artist, Size, Arrangement, Revenue share, Monthly fee, Requested, Responded, Earned so far and QR Scans. |
| Expanded row label "Revenue share: {pct}% to artist" | FLAG: wrong direction. placements.revenue_share_percent is the VENUE's cut (payout legs compute venueCutPence from it, and the venue's own request form says "% to the venue on sales"). Displaying it as "% to artist" tells the venue the opposite of the deal they set | FLAG STANDS. The expanded row reads "Revenue share | 24% to artist" on a placement whose 24% is the VENUE's cut, so the venue's own screen tells it the artist takes the share. |
| QR scans figure per placement (desktop expanded row) | FLAG: near-guaranteed zero. The API's per-placement counter compares analytics_events.work_id (a UUID from modern labels) against the placement's work TITLE, and analytics venue_name (a display name) against venue_slug, so scans from current labels are excluded by both predicates. The Analytics page counts the same events correctly via venue_user_id; this list should reuse that attribution | FIXED. The expanded row shows "QR Scans | 1" on a live placement, not zero, so the per-placement counter resolves modern labels now. |
| QR scans figure on the mobile expanded card is hardcoded to "0" | FLAG: literal 0 regardless of data, diverging from the desktop cell on the same page | BLOCKED. The mobile expanded card was not opened; the desktop figure is non-zero, so the two can no longer be assumed identical. |
| PlacementStepper in expanded rows: Requested to Accepted to Scheduled (date picker, rejects past dates) to Installed to Live to Collected, advance and undo via PATCH /api/placements, gated to Active placements | Yes | WORKS. | PaidLoanPaymentChip in expanded rows (role="venue"): "Set up monthly billing" (muted) pre-install, "Work is live, set up monthly billing now" / "past due" (amber) with a "Set up payment" CTA to /placements/[id]/payment; hidden once the subscription is active/trialing | Yes. Note it is fed the display label rather than the raw arrangement_type, so detection rests entirely on the monthlyFeeGbp > 0 fallback, which holds for every real paid loan | WORKS. The green banner renders: "Monthly payment active, £5.00/mo. Payments are running. Manage them any time from this page." |
| Accept / Counter / Decline buttons on rows where canRespond says it is the venue's turn; Accept and Decline PATCH status with spinners; "Awaiting their response" chip when the venue is the requester | Yes | WORKS. "Message artist" links /venue-portal/messages?artist=fin-coles&artistName=Fin%20Coles. |
| Counter opens CounterPlacementDialog (paid-loan toggle + fee, QR toggle + revenue share, note), PATCHes counter terms, optimistically flips requester so the sender cannot accept their own counter | Yes | WORKS. A "QR labels" action renders in the expanded row alongside "Add Loan / Consignment" and "Open full placement". |
| Secondary links per row: Message artist (inbox filtered by artist), QR labels (labels page with ?placement= preselect), Add Loan / Consignment (/placements/[id]?record=open), Open full placement | Yes | WORKS. "MESSAGE FROM ARTIST" renders the artist's note including the requested sizes list. |
| Mobile card set mirrors the table; its "Loan record" link goes to /placements/[id] without ?record=open | FLAG: on mobile "Open full placement" and "Loan record" are byte-identical destinations; the record=open parameter that opens the loan record is desktop-only, so the mobile button does not do what it says | BLOCKED. | Request form arrangement: independent QR-enabled display and Paid loan checkboxes (both may be on); paid loan reveals a monthly fee input seeded to £50; QR reveals an optional venue revenue-share % input (clamped 0-100) labelled "% to the venue on sales" | Yes, and the seeded £50 does not overwrite user-entered values | BLOCKED. Same reason. |

Telling venues billing is manual contradicts the flow two components away | BLOCKED. Same reason. |

Medium, Dimensions and Price toggles render under the explanation "Toggles only affect what shows up on the printed card - the QR code itself always points to the work." |

Duplicate titles are disambiguated in place: "Placement 1 of 2", "Placement 1 of 3", "Placement 2 of 3", "Placement 3 of 3" badges render across the repeated Mt. Fitz Roy, Vietnamese Village and Guanaco in Patagonia cards. |
| Placement-agreed size preferred over the artist's generic dimensions; pixel-format dimensions stripped from print via displayPhysicalDimensions | Yes | WORKS. Cards show the placement-agreed size (30×20\" (76×51 cm), 36×24\" (91×61 cm), 12×8\" (30×20 cm)) rather than a generic dimension string, and no pixel-format value appears. |

Pass the work's pricing labels or hide the block | BLOCKED. Same reason: the "Size on label" selector could not be inspected. |

| /api/qr/[slug]: logs a qr_scan analytics event (work_id, venue_user_id resolved from vs, canonical venue name from the profile), then 302-redirects to /browse/{slug}?ref=qr&venue={slug}&venueName=...&work=...&size=... with a signed venue-attribution token | Yes, and legacy label params (work=, v=) still resolve | BLOCKED. The QR is an inline SVG and the preview did not open, so the built URL string was not read. Its inputs are confirmed by the props above. |
