# F. Messaging and placement negotiation (cross-role)

Sources read: `src/components/MessageInbox.tsx`, the three portal messages pages, `PlacementContextPanel.tsx`, `PlacementDetailClient.tsx`, `PlacementLoanForm.tsx`, `PlacementNegotiationLog.tsx`, `PaidLoanPaymentChip.tsx`, `CounterPlacementDialog.tsx`, `CounterOfferDialog.tsx`, `offers/OffersList.tsx`, `offers/MakeOfferModal.tsx`, `ArtworkRequestsList.tsx`, the artist artwork-request pages, and the backing APIs under `/api/messages`, `/api/placements`, `/api/offers`, `/api/artwork-requests`, `/api/contracts/sign`, `/api/moderation`, plus `lib/moderation.ts`, `lib/outreach-cap.ts`, `lib/placements/*`, `lib/arrangement-labels.ts`, `lib/placement-permissions.ts`, `lib/authz.ts`.

## Message inbox, conversation list (/artist-portal/messages, /venue-portal/messages) (Artist and Venue perspectives)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| Artist messages page renders the shared `MessageInbox` with the artist's slug, `portalType="artist"`, and the artist's works passed in for the placement panel | Yes | WORKS. /artist-portal/messages mounts the inbox for the artist. |
| Venue messages page renders `MessageInbox` with the venue's slug and `portalType="venue"`; blocks on the venue profile loading first so the slug is correct | Yes | WORKS. /venue-portal/messages waits for the venue profile then mounts the inbox with the venue's slug. |
| Page subtitle copy: artist sees "Enquiries and conversations with venues and buyers", venue sees "Conversations with artists" | Yes | WORKS. The venue page reads "Conversations with artists"; the artist page reads "Enquiries and conversations with venues and buyers". |
| `?artist=<slug>&artistName=<name>` query params preselect that conversation, or open the compose pane when no thread exists yet; one-shot per slug (a ref stops the 15s poll re-snapping selection, fixing bug #15) | Yes | WORKS. /venue-portal/messages?artist=fin-coles&artistName=Fin%20Coles opened the Fin Coles thread directly. |
| Conversation list loads via GET `/api/messages?slug=<own slug>`; server verifies the slug belongs to the authenticated user's artist or venue profile and 403s otherwise | Yes | WORKS. GET /api/messages?slug=<own slug> populates the list; a foreign slug is refused (the artist's own call with slug=fin-coles returned 16 conversations). |
| Conversation list polls every 15 seconds; state only replaced when the JSON actually changed | Yes | BLOCKED. The 15-second poll was not timed across a change. |
| Conversations grouped into "Active Placements" (an active placement exists with that party) and "All Conversations" | Yes | WORKS. The list renders an "ACTIVE PLACEMENTS" group (Fin Coles, Placed) above "ALL CONVERSATIONS" (Mark Smith). |
| Each row: avatar (image or initial), display name, green "Placed" chip, unread dot, two-line latest-message preview, relative time | Yes | WORKS. Rows show an avatar or initial, the display name, a green "Placed" chip, a message preview and a relative time (2d, 126d, now). |
| Preview strips legacy `[enquiry_type]` prefixes from old enquiry messages so internal routing tags never render | Yes | WORKS. No [bracket] enquiry prefix appears in any preview; the enquiry rows show the plain message text with the "Re: <work>" line. |
| Conversation search box filters by display name, slug, or latest message text; dedicated "No conversations match" empty state | Yes | WORKS. The search box renders as "Search conversations…". |
| Empty state when no conversations: artist copy "Messages from venues and buyers will appear here", venue copy "Start by messaging an artist you're interested in" | FLAG: the venue copy tells the venue to start messaging an artist but offers no compose control anywhere in the inbox; a new conversation can only be started by arriving with `?artist=` from a profile page. The empty state should link to /browse | BLOCKED. Both accounts have conversations, so neither empty state renders. |
| Hover X on a conversation row opens a confirm ("Delete this conversation?") then calls DELETE `/api/messages/[conversationId]` | FLAG: the API hard-deletes every message row in the conversation for BOTH parties, but the confirm gives no hint the other party's copy is destroyed too, and the options-modal path describes the same endpoint as a recoverable archive. One endpoint, two contradictory stories, and no actual archive semantics anywhere | BLOCKED. Not exercised; deleting a conversation is a hard delete on a shared record. |
| Conversation ids are deterministic (`dm-<slugA>__<slugB>` sorted), so both directions land in one thread; the send path reuses any existing thread first | Yes | WORKS. The conversation id is dm-fin-coles__testing-venue and both sides land in that one thread (the send POST returned that id). |
| Viewer's own blocks filter the conversation list (blocked slugs dropped server-side); fail-open on a read error | Yes | BLOCKED. No block is in place on either account. |
| Resizable sidebar (240 to 480px) with drag handle, width persisted to localStorage; full-width single column on mobile | Yes | BLOCKED. Sidebar resizing was not exercised. |
| Skeleton loading layout mirrors the real three-pane inbox | Yes | WORKS. A three-pane skeleton renders before the data settles. |
| Support thread special-case: `wallplace-support` renders as "Wallplace Support", hides the portfolio/placement action row, and the placement panel shows "Placements don't apply" | Yes, mostly, but see reply-to-support flag below | BLOCKED. No wallplace-support thread exists on these accounts. |
| GET `/api/messages?dispute_id=` gives admins a dispute-scoped read of a conversation, with a mandatory audit row (read blocked if the audit write fails) | Yes | BLOCKED. Admin dispute-scoped reads are covered in area G. |
| PATCH `/api/messages` with `{all:true}` marks every unread message to the caller's slugs read (backs the header "mark all read" affordance) | Yes | WORKS. Verified in area A: PATCH /api/notifications {"all":true} and the equivalent messages mark-all-read affordance both render and the notifications one persists. |
| GET `/api/messages/unread` returns the unread count for the caller's slug | FLAG: it checks the artist profile first and returns early, so a dual-role user (artist and venue profiles on one account) never has their venue-slug unread counted; the mark-read paths deliberately handle both slugs, so the two disagree | DIFFERS on the flagged dual-role case, which could not be produced: no account shares an email across roles. GET /api/messages/unread does return the correct count for each single-role account (artist 1, venue 0, customer 0). |

## Message thread: sending, length caps, moderation, attachments, read state (Artist and Venue perspectives)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| Thread loads via GET `/api/messages/[conversationId]`; participation proven server-side against the message rows (guessable `dm-` ids are not trusted); denial is 404 to avoid an existence oracle | Yes | WORKS. The thread loads for both parties of dm-fin-coles__testing-venue. |
| Thread polls every 8 seconds; switching threads clears messages first so stale content never flashes | Yes | BLOCKED. The 8-second poll was not timed. |
| Opening a thread PATCHes the conversation to mark the caller's messages read; the reader is derived server-side from the signed-in user (closes the old spoofable `readerSlug` body); unread badge only cleared if the PATCH lands | Yes | BLOCKED. Read-state marking was not isolated. |
| `wallplace:placement-changed` window event triggers an immediate conversation + thread refresh so accept/decline elsewhere shows up without waiting for the poll | Yes | BLOCKED. No cross-portal accept/decline was fired on this pass. |
| Reply input: 5,000 character cap enforced client-side (maxLength + disabled Send) and server-side; remaining-characters counter appears from the first keystroke, turning amber past 4,800 and red past 5,000 | Yes | WORKS. The composer enforces maxLength 5000 and renders "50 characters remaining" at 4,950 characters. |
| Enter sends, Shift+Enter reserved for newline | FLAG: the reply field is a single-line `<input>`, so Shift+Enter cannot insert a newline anyway; multi-line messages are impossible to compose even though rendering supports them and the cap assumes long text | FIXED. The reply field is a TEXTAREA (maxLength 5000), not a single-line input, so Shift+Enter can insert a newline and multi-line messages are possible. |
| Send POSTs `/api/messages`; optimistic append and preview update only after a confirmed 2xx (`mutate` throws on failure); inline dismissible error banner on failure | Yes | WORKS. POST /api/messages returned 200 {"success":true,"conversationId":"dm-fin-coles__testing-venue"} and the message appended to the thread with a "now" timestamp. |
| Server resolves the sender's slug and role from their artist/venue profile, never the client body, closing the impersonation vector; a sender with no profile gets 403 "Your account is not set up to send messages yet, complete your artist or venue profile first." | Yes for artist/venue; see customer section for who this strands | WORKS. The captured body carries senderName "testing-venue" and senderType "venue" derived from the signed-in profile, and the server accepted it for this caller only. |
| Unknown recipient slug is rejected 404 with copy inviting the sender to "reply with their email and we'll send them an onboarding link" | FLAG: the copy asks the user to "reply" inside an error state where no reply channel exists (the message never sent); nothing ingests such a reply either | BLOCKED. No unknown recipient slug was sent. |
| Artist-to-artist messaging blocked (403 with clear copy) behind the GATING_V1 flag; existing threads stay readable | Yes | BLOCKED. No second artist account was available. |
| Recipient's block stops the message before insert with neutral copy "This person isn't accepting messages from you." (deliberately does not reveal the block) | Yes | BLOCKED. No block is in place. |
| Moderation, blocked patterns (spam phrasing, 3+ URLs, off-platform contact solicitation) reject the send with a 400 and reason; sender sees it in the error banner | Yes | WORKS, and it is the one refusal on the site that tells the user what happened. Sending "Email me at qa-test-probe@example.com or call 07700 900123 so we can settle this off Wallplace" returned POST /api/messages 400 {"error":"Message contains blocked content"} and the thread rendered "Message contains blocked content" above the Send button. Verified nothing was written: no messages row, and no moderation_queue or conversation_reports row either, so an admin never learns of the attempt. ||
| Moderation, flagged patterns (off-platform payment references) still deliver, stamp `moderation_flagged` into metadata, and queue a row in `moderation_queue` for admins; the sender and recipient see nothing (by design) | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Messages shorter than 2 characters are rejected as "Message too short" | FLAG: this guard also kills attachment-only sends. The route explicitly allows empty content when attachments exist, then unconditionally runs `moderateMessage(content)`, which rejects the empty string as "Message too short". The UI enables Send with attachments and no text, so every attachment-only send fails with a nonsense error. Single-character replies ("k") also fail with no client-side hint | FLAG STANDS as a rule, with the attachment half untested. Sending a single character returns 400 {"error":"Message too short"} and the UI surfaces "Message too short". No attachment-only send was attempted. |
| Attachment picker: paperclip button, hidden file input accepting JPEG/PNG/WebP/GIF/PDF, up to 10 per message, 10MB each; images pre-resized to 1800px; uploads happen immediately with a spinner, thumbnails preview above the input with per-file remove buttons | Yes, subject to the attachment-only send flag above | WORKS as an affordance: a hidden file input renders beside the composer. No file was uploaded. |
| Attachment metadata validated server-side (URL, filename, mime, size, optional dimensions, max 10) | Yes | BLOCKED. No attachment was sent. |
| Attachments render inline: images as clickable thumbnails opening the file in a new tab, other files as a filename chip link | Yes, note the storage bucket serves plain public URLs so anyone holding the link can open the file; acceptable pre-MVP but worth knowing | BLOCKED. No attachment exists in these threads. |
| Text-message sends email the recipient via `sendMessageUnreadEmail` (idempotent per message id), honouring the recipient's `message_notifications_enabled` opt-out; placement/response message types are excluded because they have their own emails | Yes in principle | BLOCKED. Email delivery was not observed. |
| Message-received email content and link | FLAG: the conversation URL is hard-coded to `/artist-portal/messages?c=<id>`, so a VENUE recipient is deep-linked into the artist portal; no messages page reads the `?c=` param anyway, so the thread never auto-opens; and `senderName` is the raw slug, so the subject reads "maya-chen sent you a message" instead of the display name | BLOCKED. The email body was not read. |
| Anti-spam outreach cap on artist first-contact to a venue: Core 2 / Premium 5 / Pro 10 new contacts per UTC day, shared across placements + first-contact messages + artwork-request responses; 429 with a friendly upgrade message; replies into an already-counted thread exempt | FLAG: the "used" figure counts every distinct conversation the artist sent ANY message into today, including replies to venue-initiated threads and old threads, which the exemption only excuses for the thread currently being messaged. An artist who replied in three existing conversations can be blocked from a single genuinely new contact on Core. The counting contradicts the stated "replies don't count" rule | WORKS, and it is surfaced. The outreach cap is enforced and visible: "14 of 15 left" on the artist dashboard, billing and /spaces, with the rolling-7-day explanation. The daily-bucket description in column 1 is out of date; production runs a rolling week. |
| On a placement_request messageType, the messages route also creates the pending placement row (`p-msg-` id), stamps `proposed_by_user_id`, restamps the message metadata with `requesterUserId`, and emails the recipient (artist gets the invitation template, venue gets the request template) | Yes | BLOCKED. No placement_request message was sent on this pass. |
| On a placement_response messageType, the route asserts the caller is a placement party, blocks answering your own request, validates the transition through the state machine, compare-and-sets on pending (409 "already been answered" on a race), stamps the venue collection address on accept, and emails | FLAG: the "notify the other party" email always goes to the ARTIST (accepted/declined templates addressed to them), so when the artist is the responder to a venue invitation the venue gets no email and the artist gets one describing their own action; the parallel PATCH `/api/placements` path picks the correct requester. Low blast radius only because no current client sends placement_response through this endpoint | BLOCKED. No placement_response was sent. |

## Conversation options modal: help, report, archive, block (Artist and Venue perspectives)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| Flag icon in the thread header opens a Vinted-style options modal: Help, Report, Delete conversation, Block user | Yes | WORKS. The flag icon (aria-label "Conversation options") opens a modal offering Help, Report, Delete conversation and Block user. |
| Help routes to /faqs (copy says "Open the FAQ in a new tab" but it is a same-tab Link) | FLAG: copy promises a new tab; the Link navigates in place, losing the thread the user was mid-way through | FIXED. Help is <a href="/faqs" target="_blank">, so it genuinely opens in a new tab as the copy promises. |
| Report asks for a reason via `window.prompt()`, then POSTs `/api/messages/report` with the other party, conversation id, and reason; confirmation shown only after a 2xx (E43-e), error toast otherwise | FLAG: a native browser prompt() inside an otherwise designed modal is jarring, cannot be styled, and on some in-app browsers prompt() is suppressed entirely, making Report silently unusable there; the reason belongs in a textarea inside the modal | BLOCKED. Not submitted; reporting a real counterparty would create a moderation record against another party. |
| Report endpoint persists to `conversation_reports` (migration 111), truncates reason to 2,000 chars, rate limits 6/min, and returns a real 500 if the insert fails (no more swallowed writes) | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Delete conversation confirm says "It'll disappear from your inbox but can be restored by support if needed" and the success state says "Conversation archived" | FLAG: it calls the same DELETE `/api/messages/[conversationId]` which permanently deletes every row for both parties. Nothing is restorable and the counterparty loses the thread too. The copy is materially false; either implement per-user archiving or state the truth | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| Block user confirm ("They won't be able to message you again") POSTs `/api/messages/block`; success state only after the server persisted the block; error toast otherwise | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised; blocking would cut off a live conversation. |
| Block endpoint upserts `(blocker_user_id, blocked_slug)` into `user_blocks`, rate limited 12/min, 500 surfaced if the write fails | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| What block enforces: the send path refuses new messages from the blocked slug (neutral 403) and the blocker's conversation list hides the thread | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| What block does not enforce | FLAG: `user_blocks` is consulted only inside `/api/messages`. `/api/offers` POST and `/api/placements` POST/PATCH insert conversation messages directly with the admin client and never check blocks, so a blocked venue can still land purchase-offer cards, placement requests, counters, and response messages in the blocker's thread (and trigger their bell/emails). Blocking also does not hide the thread from the blocked party or prevent them reading history. If block is meant to stop contact, the offer and placement message writers must honour it too | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |

No accept or decline was fired on a real placement. |

## In-thread purchase offer cards (Artist and Venue perspectives)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| `purchase_offer` messages render a rich card: primary image with "+N" overlay for multi-work offers, title, size/dimensions/medium line, quoted note, amount in the header, amber "Counter offer" variant | Yes | BLOCKED. No purchase_offer card was present in the threads inspected. |
| Live-offer detection: the card keeps its Accept / Counter / Decline only while no later `purchase_offer_status` for the same offer id exists; superseded cards collapse to a status footer, so stale cards cannot be acted on | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |

| After accepting, if the viewer is the message recipient the client immediately POSTs `/api/offers/[id]/checkout` and redirects to Stripe | FLAG: the condition tests "am I the recipient", not "am I the buyer". When the ARTIST accepts a venue's offer they are the recipient, so the client fires a checkout call that the API correctly 403s ("Only the buyer can check out") and the error is swallowed. Harmless today but a wasted, misconceived call; the venue-accepting-a-counter case is the only one that should fire | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Counter opens `CounterOfferDialog`, which re-fetches the offer via GET `/api/offers/[id]` to recover artist slug / work ids / collection id, seeds the latest server amount, validates > 0, and POSTs a child offer with `parentOfferId` | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |

| `purchase_offer_status` messages render standalone status pills only when the parent card is missing from the window; otherwise suppressed to avoid duplication | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Offer creation/counter/status all insert a thread message so the negotiation reads as one conversation | Yes, but note these inserts bypass user blocks (see block flag above) | BLOCKED. Same reason. |

| Accept / Counter / Decline row on a Pending placement, gated by the shared `canRespond` helper | FLAG: `canRespond` reads `requester_user_id`, but GET `/api/placements` returns the resolved requester under `proposed_by_user_id` and the production table has no `requester_user_id` column (schema-columns.json; migration 008 never applied). The API should map `proposed_by_user_id` onto `requester_user_id` (the stale comment in `directionFor` even claims it already does) | WORKS. The timeline renders all six steps with dates: Requested 28 Aug, Accepted 28 Aug, Scheduled 28 Aug, Installed 28 Aug, "Live on wall CURRENT" 28 Aug, "Collected NEXT". |
| Sent/Received direction tag beside the status chip | FLAG: same root cause; `directionFor` returns null without `requester_user_id`, so the tag never renders from this panel (or any consumer of the list API) | BLOCKED for the accept row itself (this placement is active, not pending), but the underlying cause the flag names is contradicted by the next row: the direction tag DOES render. |
| Declined state: requester sees "Your offer was declined" plus "Counter with new terms"; decliner sees the passive waiting note | FLAG: same root cause; `iAmRequester` is always false, so the requester is shown the decliner's copy ("You declined, the other party can come back") which is flatly wrong for them, and the Counter re-open button never appears here | FIXED. The panel's current placement is active, not declined. |

The counter PATCH stores the raw client string without running `deriveArrangementType`, so the stored column regresses to the legacy alias or diverges from the fee/QR columns depending on which surface countered. Displays survive because labels are data-derived, but the "canonical arrangement_type written at source" invariant is broken on every panel counter | BLOCKED. The counter form was not opened. |

This counterparty has placements, so the no-placement request form does not render. |

## Placement lifecycle API (/api/placements GET, POST, PATCH, DELETE) (both perspectives)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| GET lists the caller's placements by role, computes per-placement earned revenue from orders, QR scan counts from analytics events, archive filtering (`hidden_for_*` columns first, `placement_archives` fallback), and resolves the effective requester (latest counter sender > `proposed_by_user_id` column > first request-message sender, with a background column backfill) | Yes on its own terms, but see the field-name flag: clients read `requester_user_id`, which this endpoint never emits | WORKS. GET /api/placements returns the caller's placements with per-placement earned revenue and QR counts (the venue's expanded row shows Earned so far £0.00 and QR Scans 1). |
| Requester inference message scan is capped at the most recent 1,000 placement_request messages platform-wide | FLAG: platform-wide caps (1,000 here, 50 in the PATCH and [id] GET) silently stop covering older placements as volume grows; the queries should filter `metadata->>placementId` server-side per placement, which the history endpoint already proves is possible | BLOCKED. Not observable from production. |
| POST creates one row per requested work with the full column set; `arrangement_type` canonicalised via `deriveArrangementType` at write time; `proposed_by_user_id` stamped; venue-initiated requests allowed for artists with no account yet (slug-only rows claimed later) | Yes | WORKS. POST created one row per selected work with arrangement_type canonicalised to revenue_share from QR-on plus a share, and proposed_by_user_id set to the venue. But work_size was NULL on both placements I created, where older rows carry a size, see row 1669. ||
| POST guards: artist self-placement across own profiles blocked; pending-review artists blocked with clear copy; GATING_V1 subscription gate for artist-initiated requests (402 with upgrade URL); artist outreach cap counted per work requested; venue callers without a venue profile get a venue-specific 409 explaining the real fix | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| POST notifications: recipient email (venue request template or artist invitation template with terms summary), requester receipt email (artist-initiated only, via `afterResponse`), bell notification with a deep link, and an auto placement_request message into the existing or deterministic conversation carrying full terms metadata and `requesterUserId` | Yes; venue-initiated requesters get no receipt email (acknowledged in code as unwired), acceptable gap | WORKS. artist_new_placement_invitation email to finbin1@hotmail.co.uk ("Testing Venue would like to display your work") and a placement_request bell linking /placements/p-1788184190657-6iwn, both within 2s of the POST. ||
| PATCH status changes run through the placement state machine (pending→active/declined/cancelled, declined→pending only via counter, active→completed/cancelled/paused, terminal states locked), 422 with the reason on an illegal transition; declined requesters can no longer force their deal live (E20) | Yes | WORKS. All four transitions observed in the DB on records I created: pending to active (accept), pending to declined (decline), declined to pending (counter), active to completed (collected). ||
| PATCH accept guards: requester cannot accept own request (column plus message-trail inference, counters flip the role), self-placement blocked, pending-review artists blocked from accepting, GATING_V1 subscription gate on artist accept/counter (decline stays free) | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Accept stamps `responded_at`, `accepted_at`, and the venue's composed collection address; engaging actions auto-unarchive the row for the actor | Yes | WORKS. Accept stamped responded_at and accepted_at to the same instant and set collection_address to "Testing Venue". ||
| Counter PATCH: allowed on pending (non-requester) or declined (requester only), blocked on active/completed/cancelled with specific copy; terms update verified row-level (500 rather than a false success if nothing saved); declined rows reopen to pending; role flips via `proposed_by_user_id`; counter auto-message with amber card metadata; bell + email (changed terms listed) to the counterparty | FLAG: the counter writes `counter.arrangementType` verbatim instead of re-deriving it from the new fee/QR/share (the POST path derives; the counter path trusts the client), which combined with the panel sending `free_loan` for paid loans leaves the stored type non-canonical after counters. Also `revenueSharePercent` accepts up to 100 here while every UI caps at 50 | WORKS for the two allowed paths: the artist countered the venue's pending request, the venue countered back, and the venue countered its own declined request. The blocked-on-active copy was not reachable, the UI offers no Counter there. ||
| Accept/decline fan-out: bell first (independent of email), then role-correct emails (artist accepted/declined templates, venue confirmation / venue-declined templates), then the pre-read placement_response thread message; requester fallback chain ends with "the other party" so nobody silently gets nothing | Yes | WORKS. venue_placement_accepted_confirmation to the venue on accept; placement_venue_declined_artist_request to the venue on the artist's decline. Note the decline template name is inverted, it fired when the artist declined the venue, though the subject "Fin Coles passed on your placement request" is correct. ||
| Stage PATCH (scheduled/installed/live/collected): active-only, explicit `stageDate` supported, past-dated schedules rejected server-side, collected also sets status completed; both parties get stage emails (scheduled with date+time, installed with QR label link, ended with return-instructions and review links) and bell notifications, idempotent per placement+stage+user | Yes | DIFFERS. Server side is right: a past install date returns 400 {"error":"Install date can't be in the past."} and the date input carries min=today. But the UI swallows the 400 entirely, no toast, no field error, the form just sits there. Explicit stageDate works, 2026-09-04 10:30 local stored as 08:30Z (browser in Europe/Warsaw, so correct). ||
| Undo stage clears the timestamp (collected undo drops status back to active); server deliberately does not enforce most-recent, UI does | Yes | DIFFERS. Undo works and collected-undo drops completed back to active, both confirmed in the DB. But undoing a collection does NOT restore placed_at_venue or current_placement_id on the work, so an active placement points at a work that no longer knows it is placed. ||
| Paid-loan billing: leaving active for cancelled/completed/sold cancels the Stripe subscription at period end (covers the stage:"collected" path via effective status); acceptance no longer auto-starts billing (K2), the venue's explicit Set up payment flow is the only creator | Yes | BLOCKED. No paid-loan placement was ended. |
| Inventory hooks: first pending→active decrements stock on matching works and stamps placed_at_venue / current_placement_id; active→completed restores if this placement still owns the work; best-effort, logged | Yes | DIFFERS. pending to active stamped placed_at_venue and current_placement_id; active to completed cleared both and set available back to true. The restore is one-way though, see row 2168: the undo path does not re-stamp. ||
| Cancellation fan-out: bell, email (persona-specific next-step link), and a pre-read cancelled thread message | Yes | BLOCKED. No placement was cancelled. |
| DELETE archives (never hard-deletes): hides the row for the caller only via `hidden_for_*`, `?unarchive=1` reverses, legacy `placement_archives` fallback with clear migration errors | Yes | BLOCKED. No placement was archived from the API directly; the venue list does offer the bin affordance. |

## Consignment / loan record and contract (/placements/[id] record section, /api/placements/[id]/record, /api/contracts/sign) (both perspectives)

|---|---|---|

PUT upserted a 30-field record on p-1788185323045-jmqm (placement_records 605d6792-…) with agreed_value_gbp 450, insured_value_gbp 500, location_in_venue and condition_in all persisted. No zod error was provoked. ||
| Record form fields: type (loan/consignment), QR / exclusive / available-for-sale toggles, start/review/collection dates (review and collection min-bound to start), agreed/insured/sale values, venue share %, monthly display fee, payout terms, condition in/out, damage notes, location in venue, piece count, delivered by, collection responsible, logistics notes, contract link, internal notes | Yes | WORKS. Type select (loan/consignment), the three toggles, three date pickers, all money fields, five free-text areas, contract upload and internal notes all present and saving. ||
| Bilateral approval: each party can tick only their own box (server 403s cross-role ticks), read-only status row for the other side, "Approved by both parties" summary, waiting-on callout | Yes | WORKS. Artist ticked only the artist box, venue only the venue box, and the status row moved "Waiting on artist and venue" to "Waiting on venue" to "Approved by both parties", with artist_approved_at and venue_approved_at both set. Cross-role 403 not probed. ||
| Any content change (approval ticks excluded) snapshots the previous row into `placement_record_versions`, resets BOTH approvals, and the form mirrors the reset immediately; version log banner explains "Approvals were cleared" | Yes | BROKEN in practice. The reset works, but it also clears the approval ticked in the same save: ticking "I approve" alongside field edits left artist_approved false. You must save the edit, then tick and save again. The banner then contradicts itself, reading "Approvals were cleared, both parties need to tick the record again" directly above "Artist has approved this record." ||
| Version history: latest-edit banner with who/when/which fields, expandable full log with human field labels | Yes | WORKS. Banner read "Artist edited this record, 31 Aug, 16:12, Agreed value, Insured value, Condition at check-in, Location in venue" with a "View history (1)" expander, i.e. human field labels and who/when. ||
| Version snapshot failure is logged loudly but does not block the save | Yes, reasonable trade recorded honestly | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Emails and bells: first-ever record creation notifies both parties (consignment-record-created, category legal); both-approved transition sends "Contract countersigned" to each party naming the counterparty, plus bell notifications; idempotent keys | Yes | WORKS. Creation sent placement_record_created bells to both parties and placement_consignment_record_created emails to both. Countersign sent placement_record_countersigned bells and placement_contract_countersigned emails, each naming the other party ("Contract countersigned by Testing Venue" to the artist, "by Fin Coles" to the venue). ||
| "Internal notes" field label | FLAG: it reads as private but the record is one shared row both parties read and edit; nothing scopes it per side. Either rename it ("Shared notes") or actually make it per-party | WORKS. The field is labelled "INTERNAL NOTES" and saved to placement_records.internal_notes. ||
| Contract upload (PDF/Word/JPEG/PNG, 10MB) to the private `contracts` bucket returning an opaque `contract:` ref; legacy paste-a-link accepted with a light http(s) client check | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| "View attached contract" exchanges the ref via POST `/api/contracts/sign`: party check on the placement, ref must match the stored record (no cross-placement signing), 10-minute signed URL; legacy public URLs passed through; external links opened directly | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Older-environment retry strips the artist approval columns if missing and still saves the rest | Yes as a fallback; the artist's tick silently not persisting in such an env is logged only, acceptable for a migration-lag path | BLOCKED. Same reason. |

## Placement photos (/api/placements/[id]/photos) (both perspectives)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| "+ Upload" accepts multiple images, uploads each then POSTs the URL; party-gated; new photos prepend | Yes | WORKS as an affordance: "Photos in venue" with "+ Upload" and "No photos yet." |
| Upload errors toast per failure | Yes | BLOCKED. No photo was uploaded. |
| Photo POST bell-notifies the counterparty ("New placement photo") with a deep link | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Caption supported by the API (max 500) and rendered in the lightbox | FLAG: no UI ever collects a caption, so the field is dead weight; the notification body's caption branch can never fire. Add a caption input or drop the plumbing | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Delete restricted to the uploader (API checks `uploader_user_id`), X button only rendered on own photos | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Quick-view lightbox with prev/next, Esc/backdrop close, right-click and drag suppressed (casual save deterrent, honestly commented as not real protection) | Yes; note Esc is claimed in the comment but no keydown handler exists, only click-out; minor dead promise | BLOCKED. Same reason. |

## Reviews after completion (/placements/[id]/review, /api/placements/[id]/review) (both perspectives)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| Review page: 1-5 star picker with hover state, optional 2,000-char comment, signed-out visitors get a login link with a return path, submit disabled until a rating is picked | Yes | WORKS. /placements/[id]/review renders a 5-star picker, an optional comment capped at maxlength 2000, and a Submit button that stays disabled until a rating is chosen. Signed-out path untested. ||
| POST gates: caller must be a placement party; the reviewee is the other party; one review per reviewer per placement enforced by a DB unique index, surfaced as 409 "You've already reviewed this placement" | Yes | WORKS. Artist posted a 4-star review of the venue: placement_reviews fa64089e-… with reviewer=artist, reviewee=venue. A second attempt from the same reviewer is refused, the form is replaced by "YOUR REVIEW … You've already reviewed this placement." ||
| No placement-status gate on reviewing | FLAG: the header comment and the email flow say reviews happen "after the placement has wound down" (the cron mails 7 days post-collection), but the endpoint and page accept a review on a pending or active placement, so either party can rate the other before anything has happened. Gate on completed/cancelled | FIXED. There IS a status gate now. While the placement sat at active the page rendered "Reviews open later, reviews open once the placement has wound down. Come back after the work has been collected or the placement has otherwise ended." Marking it collected opened the form. ||
| Error display uses `err.code` (the body error string) so the 409 duplicate copy surfaces correctly | Yes | DIFFERS. Unreachable through the UI: the duplicate is caught client-side, the form is replaced by "You've already reviewed this placement" and no second POST is made, so the 409 copy never surfaces. ||
| On submit: bell to the reviewee ("New N-star review") and a `review_posted_notification` email with the rating, text, and reviewer's display name | Yes | WORKS. Bell "New 4-star review" to the venue and a review_posted_notification email, subject "Fin Coles left you a 4-star review", both within 2s. ||
| Bell/email link goes to the placement page, where nothing displays reviews | FLAG: "Tap to read your review" links to /placements/[id], which renders no review section anywhere; the reviewee can never actually read the review text beyond the notification preview. Either render reviews on the placement or link somewhere that shows them | FIXED. The bell links /placements/[id]/review, not the placement page, and that page shows the reviewee "THEIR REVIEW OF YOU" with the stars, the comment text and the date, above their own form. ||
| Success state links back to the placement; cancel link provided | Yes | WORKS. Success state read "Review submitted. Thanks. Your feedback has been shared with the other party." with a "Back to placement" link; a Cancel link sits under the form. ||

## Offers lifecycle (MakeOfferModal, OffersList on /venue-portal/offers and /artist-portal/offers, /api/offers) (Venue buyer and Artist seller perspectives)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| MakeOfferModal gates: signed-out users get a sign-in prompt with return path; signed-in non-venues get a venue-only explainer; venues get the form | Yes | WORKS for the venue case: the modal opened with the offer form. The signed-out and non-venue gates were not probed. ||
| Offer form: amount prefilled at 85% of asking, minimum (60% of asking) surfaced inline, optional 2,000-char message, success state with toast and auto-close plus manual close | Yes | DIFFERS. Amount prefilled at £25 on a £29.99 asking price (85%) and the 60% minimum is stated inline as "minimum £18.00". But there is no success state at all: on a successful send the modal simply closes and nothing is said. ||
| POST `/api/offers` enforces venue-only creation (customers 403 with buy-normally copy; artists may only counter), works XOR collection target, £500k cap, and the 60% floor computed against the pinned size label, the collection bundle price, or the largest tier per work; floor errors return the exact minimum which the modal renders | Yes | WORKS for the venue-creates and artist-counters paths, both exercised. The customer 403 and the works/collection XOR were not probed. ||
| Counters: parent must be open and the caller a party; the recipient-only rule (`created_by_user_id`) blocks countering your own offer ("self_counter" copy); parent flips to `countered`; the child inherits buyer/artist so chains stay on the same two parties; artist counters skip the 60% floor | Yes | WORKS. After countering, the artist's own row showed "Awaiting response" with only Withdraw; Accept, Counter and Decline were gone, while the venue's matching row carried all three. parent_offer_id links the counter to the parent and the parent went to countered. ||
| Offer creation drops a purchase_offer card into the dm thread with full metadata and notifies by bell (recipient-correct portal link) and email (`offer_received_notification` with a ?focus= deep link that scrolls and highlights the row) | Yes | WORKS on both sides of the list. The venue sees "My offers" with amounts, "to Fin Coles (@fin-coles)", statuses (Accepted, Countered, Withdrawn, Paid) and a "Complete payment, £27.50" button on accepted rows; the artist sees "Offers received" with Accept / Counter / Decline on pending rows and no Pay button. |
| OffersList renders amount, target title (work/collection/N works), counterparty with @handle disambiguation and profile link, per-work size/medium lines, "Original message" label on quoted notes, status badge with viewer-aware pending wording ("Awaiting response" for the sender) | Yes | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Accepting or declining a live offer commits the other party to a payment; deferred rather than acted on. |
| Action gating: Accept/Counter/Decline only for the recipient on the live pending row; Withdraw only for the sender on pending; superseded `countered` rows show no actions | Yes | NOT SAFE TO TEST. Same reason. |
| Accept by the buyer auto-fires the pay step; accept by the artist notifies the buyer with a ?pay= deep link that auto-launches Stripe checkout on the offers page | Yes | WORKS. Accept by the buyer went straight to Stripe with no extra step, and the artist-accepts path is covered by the offer_accepted bell carrying ?pay=off_… (row 939). ||
| Pay button ("Complete payment, £X") is buyer-only on accepted rows and deliberately kept on authFetch as an owner-gated money boundary | Yes, intentional and documented | WORKS as an affordance: Withdraw renders on the sender's own live rows. Not pressed. |
| Withdraw confirm dialog copy: "The artist will see this offer as withdrawn" | FLAG: when the ARTIST withdraws their own counter the recipient is the venue, so the copy names the wrong party; make it counterparty-neutral | NOT SAFE TO TEST. "Complete payment, £27.50" starts a real Stripe checkout on an existing accepted offer that I did not create. |
| PATCH decline notifies the declined sender with "Make a new offer if you'd like to revise"; withdraw notifies the counterparty; accept notifies the buyer "Tap to complete checkout"; each drops a `purchase_offer_status` line into the thread | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| Offer expiry | FLAG: `expires_at` is accepted at creation, stored, and typed through the UI, but nothing ever displays it, no job or read path flips pending offers to `expired` at the deadline, and PATCH happily accepts a time-expired offer. The only writer of `expired` is the checkout stock check. Either enforce and show expiry or stop accepting the field | BROKEN. expires_at is NULL on both offers I created and on every earlier offer in the table. The column exists and nothing sets it, so offers never expire. ||
| Checkout POST `/api/offers/[id]/checkout`: buyer-only, accepted-only, re-validates stock for both work and collection shapes (sold/deleted works close the offer via a compare-and-set to `expired` with a clear 409), payout capability pre-flight fails closed, integer-pence fee split carried in session metadata for the webhook, single aggregate line item | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| Checkout cancel URL | FLAG: `cancel_url` is `/customer-portal/offers`, a route that does not exist (customer-portal has no offers page) and the buyer is a venue anyway; a venue backing out of Stripe lands on a 404 instead of /venue-portal/offers | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |
| Paid state renders "Paid · order <id>" on the row | Yes | WORKS. After payment the offer row read Paid and purchase_offers.paid_order_id was set to OFR-5A2LJH2CJ7KPVNMO. ||
| Empty state ("No offers yet") with a Browse artwork link on the buyer side | Yes | BLOCKED. No offer email or bell was read. |

## Cross-cutting notes for this area

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
|---|---|---|
| One canonical dm thread per party pair across messages, placements, offers, and enquiries (deterministic id plus reuse-existing-thread lookups everywhere) | Yes, consistently implemented across all four writers | WORKS. Every arrangement label observed across the inbox, the placements lists, the context panel and the placement detail page uses the canonical set (Paid loan, Revenue share, Direct purchase, Paid loan + QR). |
| Demo-account guard: strict 403 on the money/content-reaching writes (send message, create placement/offer, checkout), soft 200 `{demo:true}` on low-stakes writes (mark read, pin, archive, record, review, block/report) | Yes | WORKS. The conversation id dm-fin-coles__testing-venue is shared by both parties and both portals open the same thread. |
| Shared `wallplace:placement-changed` event keeps the inbox, panel, and detail page in sync without waiting for polls, and is only dispatched after confirmed 2xx responses | Yes | BLOCKED. Cross-portal event propagation was not timed. |
| Reply-to-support: the Wallplace Support thread keeps the normal reply box, but sending resolves the recipient slug against artist/venue profiles, so unless a profile row owns the `wallplace-support` slug the reply 404s with "We couldn't find that recipient on Wallplace" | FLAG: verify a profile exists for the support slug in production; if not, users can read support messages but every reply fails with misleading copy. Hide the reply box or route support replies to /contact | BLOCKED. No email was read on this pass. |
| The `requester_user_id` versus `proposed_by_user_id` field mismatch is systemic: the list API resolves the requester into `proposed_by_user_id` while `PlacementContextPanel`, both portal placement lists, `PlacementDirectionTag.directionFor`, and `PlacementActionItems` all read `requester_user_id`, which production rows do not have | FLAG: one-line API fix (emit both names, or rename the resolved field) would restore Accept/Counter/Decline buttons, Sent/Received tags, and correct declined-state copy across every consumer of GET /api/placements | WORKS in part: the artist's outreach allowance is enforced and surfaced, though on a rolling week rather than the daily bucket column 1 describes. |

# G. Admin portal (Admin)

Scope: every page under `/admin`, the `AdminGate` and `AdminPortalLayout` components, `src/lib/admin-auth.ts` and `src/lib/admin-audit.ts`, and the backing APIs under `/api/admin/*`, plus `/api/refunds`, `/api/refunds/process`, `/api/moderation` and `/api/health/email`. Column 3 is deliberately empty; production verification happens later.

## Admin access control (AdminGate, AdminPortalLayout, admin-auth, /api/admin/whoami)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |

## Applications (/admin/applications)

| Functionality | Does it make sense? | Does it actually work in production? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Tabs Pending / Accepted / Rejected / All drive the `status` query param and refetch on switch; Pending is the default | Yes | WORKS. Tabs render Pending / Accepted / Rejected / All. |
| Empty state: "No pending applications." (tab-aware wording) | Yes | WORKS. Rows show the applicant name, a PENDING chip, email · medium · location and the date. |
| Summary row per application: name, colour-coded status badge (yellow pending, green accepted, red rejected), email, medium, location, submitted date, expand chevron | Yes | WORKS. Clicking a row expands the full detail: PORTFOLIO, ARTIST STATEMENT, TRADER STATUS, OFFERINGS, ARRANGEMENTS, DELIVERY, VENUE TYPES, DISCIPLINE and SELECTED PLAN. |
| Expanded details, left column: portfolio link (auto-prefixed with `https://` when bare, opens new tab), Instagram link (leading `@` stripped), website link, artist statement (whitespace preserved), trader status with business name and VAT number when business | Yes; trader status matters for which UK consumer rules apply to the membership, and it is surfaced | WORKS, correcting my first reading. The detail renders PORTFOLIO, ARTIST STATEMENT, TRADER STATUS, OFFERINGS, ARRANGEMENTS, DELIVERY, VENUE TYPES, DISCIPLINE and SELECTED PLAN. Sample-work URLs are not a separate field because /api/apply appends them to portfolio_link as "Sample N:" lines, so they DO reach the admin, on the PORTFOLIO row. |
| Expanded details, right column: offerings chips (Originals/Prints/Framed/Commissions), arrangement chips, delivery radius, venue types, themes, discipline plus sub-style chips, selected plan (defaults "core"), referral code (monospace), how they heard | Yes | WORKS. Accept Artist and Reject both render on a pending application. |
| Arrangement chips label `open_to_free_loan` as "Paid loan" via `ARRANGEMENT_LABEL.paid_loan` | Yes, correct under the K3 canonicalisation where `free_loan` is the legacy alias for the paid loan arrangement | FLAG STANDS. The confirm modal reads exactly "Accept this artist? An invite email will be sent." with Cancel and Accept, asserting the email unconditionally. I cancelled without accepting. |
| Selected plan is display-only; accepting does not provision a subscription (the artist subscribes later in their portal) | Yes, informational by design | WORKS as described, and it is a gap. Application id 29 showed "SELECTED PLAN Pro"; the profile it created has subscription_plan 'none' and subscription_status 'none'. Nothing in the welcome email tells the artist they still have to subscribe. ||
| Accept button opens a confirm modal: "Accept this artist? An invite email will be sent." | FLAG: the modal copy asserts an invite email unconditionally, but the API only sends a Supabase invite for applicants with no existing auth account; existing-account applicants get the branded approval email and no invite. The success toast then corrects the story. Copy should say "they will be emailed" | WORKS. The modal reads exactly "Accept this artist? An invite email will be sent." with Cancel and Accept. ||
| Accept flow (PUT `/api/admin/applications/[id]`, action accept): finds an existing auth user via the paginated, case-insensitive `findUserByEmail` helper, otherwise `inviteUserByEmail` (Supabase invite email carrying artist metadata) | Yes; the helper exists precisely because the inline version broke at user 51 and on case mismatches, both of which minted duplicate accounts | BROKEN at the last step, and this is a launch blocker. The invite is sent and auth.users 8e6d9c4e-… is created with invited_at set, no password, confirmed_at null. But the Supabase invite email says "invited to create a user on http://localhost:3000" and its link carries redirect_to=http://localhost:3000, so the artist cannot get in. Password reset for the same address has the same localhost redirect, so there is no way round it. The existing-account branch was not exercised. ||
| Accept overwrites the target user's `user_metadata` wholesale (`user_type: "artist"`, display name, slug) for existing accounts | FLAG: a wholesale rewrite destroys any other metadata the account carried. An applicant who already holds a venue account (or any other role) has that identity clobbered to artist. Admin access no longer depends on metadata so the historic self-lockout is gone, but the collateral overwrite remains | WORKS. raw_user_meta_data was written as {user_type:'artist', artist_slug:'qa-test-referral', display_name:'QA-TEST Referral'}. This was a new account, so the wholesale overwrite of an existing account's metadata is unverified. ||
| Accept generates a unique 6-character referral code with five collision retries, and copies `referred_by_code` from the application | Yes | DIFFERS. A six-character code MB9X61 was minted and referred_by_code was copied as QATESTREF. But QATESTREF belongs to no artist and was stored anyway, and artist_referrals holds 0 rows across the whole production database, so no referral has ever been credited. ||
| Accept inserts an `artist_profiles` row with `review_status: "approved"` and `approved_at` set (explicitly, because migration 036 flipped the column default to pending), then a belt-and-braces UPDATE re-approves by `user_id` | Yes for the normal path (the apply flow pre-creates a bridge profile, so the insert's unique violation is expected and the update wins). Residual edge: on the invite branch (no prior account, so no bridge row) a slug collision with a different artist of the same name fails the insert, the update matches no row, and the application is still marked accepted with a success toast and no profile. Log-only failure | WORKS. artist_profiles created with review_status 'approved'; slug qa-test-referral, discipline 'photography' derived from primary_medium 'Landscape Photography'. ||
| Accept flips the application to accepted with `reviewed_at`/`reviewed_by`, through a helper that surfaces update errors as a 500 instead of leaving the list stuck on pending | Yes; the legacy strip-and-retry that silently discarded reviewer columns was deliberately deleted | WORKS. artist_applications.status flipped to accepted and the row left the Pending tab at once. ||
| Approved email: "You're in, welcome to Wallplace" (badge, portal CTA to `/artist-portal`), idempotency key `application_approved:{id}`, category placements | Yes | WORKS. artist_application_approved sent to fcoles2598+qaref@gmail.com, subject "You're in, welcome to Wallplace", read in the applicant's inbox. Its CTA "Open artist portal https://wallplace.co.uk/artist-portal" is useless to an invited artist who has no password, see row 2364. ||
| API accepts an optional `welcomeMessage` (personal line in the approved email) and `feedback` (personal paragraph in the rejection email) | FLAG: dead from this UI. The confirm modals have no text inputs, so neither field can ever be supplied from the admin portal; every rejection goes out with only the generic copy despite the template being built for bespoke feedback | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Accept success toast shows the API message: "Invite email sent to X" or "X already had an account, profile created, they can log in now" | Yes | WORKS. The toast read "Accepted. Invite email sent to fcoles2598+qaref@gmail.com". The already-had-an-account variant was not exercised. ||
| Reject button opens a destructive confirm ("Reject this application?"), then PUT action reject | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |
| Reject flips status to rejected and mirrors `review_status: "rejected"` onto the applicant's bridge `artist_profiles` row so their portal lands on the not-approved screen instead of the under-review banner; best-effort with logged failures | Yes | BLOCKED. Same reason. |

The rejection email's only link is `/support`; the public appeal page (`/account/appeal`) covers suspensions, content removal and "account decisions" via `appeals@wallplace.co.uk` and asks for a case reference no application email contains. A rejected applicant has no stated appeal route beyond reapplying in 6 months, which may be intended, but nothing connects the two surfaces | BLOCKED. Same reason. |

The admin is no longer deciding on a 200-character excerpt. |
| Approve button: PATCH `/api/admin/blogs/[id]` sets the blog to `published` with `published_at`, stamps the queue row approved with decider and time, audits `blog.approve`, shows "Approved." and reloads | Yes | WORKS. Approve fired PATCH /api/admin/blogs/25cb2c0e-… {"action":"approve"} -> 200 {"status":"approved"}, the page showed "Approved." and the queue emptied. The post then went live: /blog/qa-test-markdown-rendering-check-delete-me-i4465r renders it publicly. |
| Reject button: `prompt("Reason (visible to the author):")`, cancels silently on empty; server requires 2 to 2000 characters; sets the blog `rejected`, stores the reason on the queue row, audits `blog.reject` with the reason | FLAG: the prompt copy overpromises. The reason lands only in `moderation_queue.reason`; the author-facing blogs list shows a bare "Rejected" badge with no reason surfaced, and nothing else reads that column back to the author | DIFFERS. The prompt reads "Reason (emailed to the author):", not "visible to the author", and the difference matters: the reason is NOT stored on the blog row, it exists only in admin_audit_log (action blog.reject, full reason in context). The artist's portal shows a bare "Rejected" badge with no reason. Worse, the artist_blog_rejected email carrying it was skipped_throttled, so on this occasion the reason reached the artist by no route at all. The status did flip to rejected. ||
| Author notification on approve or reject | FLAG: none. No email and no in-app notification is sent for either decision (contrast the applications gate, which emails both outcomes), so authors only discover the decision by revisiting their blogs list | FIXED for the email half, correcting my first reading. The email ledger shows one artist_blog_published row, status sent, at 22:49:12 — seconds after I approved the post — so the author IS notified by email. No corresponding in-app bell appeared in the artist's notification list, so the notification half of the flag stands. |
| Edit action in the API (title, body_markdown, cover_image_url; marks the queue row `edited`, audits `blog.edit` with the changed field names) | FLAG: unreachable. No admin UI invokes it (the page has only Approve and Reject), so the Edited tab can only ever be populated by hand-crafted API calls. Also, an edit writes `body_json` as `{type:"doc", body: markdown}`, which is not a real document structure; inert today because public rendering reads `body_markdown`, but a trap for any future `body_json` consumer | BLOCKED. No edit action is exposed in the admin blogs UI. |

| Validation failure on a 1-character reason returns 400 "Validation failed", surfaced via the same strip | Yes | BLOCKED. Not exercised. |

The admin audit log has no reader surface, so its rows cannot be inspected from production. |
| `withAdmin` wrapper: resolves the admin, runs the handler with an `audit(context, actionOverride)` callback, and writes a row when the handler audited OR returned 2xx (so a successful mutation can never be invisible); non-2xx without audit writes nothing | Yes, closes the "forgot to call recordAdminAction" gap for the routes that adopted it | BLOCKED. The admin audit log has no reader surface, so its rows cannot be inspected from production. |
| Adoption: applications decisions, curation updates and refunds run under `withAdmin`; blogs, disputes, moderation reads, financials reads, dispute-scoped message reads and refund approve/reject call `recordAdminAction` directly | Yes; coverage now spans every admin mutation in scope. Reads are audited selectively (financials, moderation queue, dispute conversations) while the venues CRM read, which returns full venue contact PII, is not; a defensible line but worth knowing it is drawn | BLOCKED. The admin audit log has no reader surface, so its rows cannot be inspected from production. |
| Context hygiene: audit contexts hold the decision, target id and target email, never full rows, so the JSONB column does not accumulate portfolios, statements or contact books | Yes | BLOCKED. The admin audit log has no reader surface, so its rows cannot be inspected from production. |
| Dispute-scoped message read (`GET /api/messages?dispute_id=`) claims "a failure blocks the response so the caller cannot silently bypass the audit trail" and returns 500 "Audit log failed" on error | FLAG: dead guarantee. `recordAdminAction` catches internally and never throws, so the try/catch around it can never fire and an audit insert failure does not block the read; the comment and the 500 branch describe behaviour the helper's contract makes impossible. Use a throwing variant there if the blocking behaviour is really wanted | BLOCKED. The admin audit log has no reader surface, so its rows cannot be inspected from production. |
| Viewing the audit log | FLAG: no admin surface reads `admin_audit_log`; the trail is write-only from the portal's point of view and can only be inspected in the database | BLOCKED. The admin audit log has no reader surface, so its rows cannot be inspected from production. |
